Abstract (英文): Hourly satellite precipitation products are often evaluated using aggregate statistics that can conceal compensating errors in precipitation occurrence and event duration, accumulation, and intensity. Using all matched observations from 522 meteorological stations in North China between 1 October 2019 and 26 September 2022, this study evaluated GPM IMERG Final Run V07 and GSMaP_Gauge V8. The framework combined precipitation-amount and occurrence metrics, event characteristics defined using local 90th percentiles of wet-hour precipitation, and additive and multiplicative error decomposition. IMERG showed a persistent dry bias, with a mean annualized total error of −348.2 mm yr−1. GSMaP had a higher probability of detection than IMERG (77.6% versus 38.6%) but also a higher false alarm ratio (67.5% versus 29.7%); its mean total error of +99.7 mm yr−1 masked substantial compensation among the error components. For extreme precipitation events, IMERG underestimated annualized event frequency by 33.9% but reproduced mean duration comparatively closely (−11.0%). GSMaP overestimated event frequency by 31.6% and duration by 198.6%. Its near-unbiased mean event total (+1.1%) resulted from prolonged duration despite underestimation of mean and peak intensity by 67.3% and 67.4%, respectively. Within the approximately three-year evaluation period examined here, these results show that product selection should be application-specific and that event-scale correction should address precipitation occurrence, duration, accumulation, and intensity jointly.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Building change detection (BCD) in high-resolution remote sensing imagery has achieved considerable accuracy with deep learning, yet most existing models still suffer from performance degradation under noise interference, partly due to inadequate uncertainty handling. To address this, we propose GUF-Net, a gated uncertainty-aware fusion network that integrates three key components: (1) a pixel-wise uncertainty modeling module that generates a pixel-level uncertainty map alongside the change probability map; (2) an adaptive gated fusion mechanism that dynamically weights multi-scale features and uncertainty information; and (3) a redesigned loss function that can directly supervise the uncertainty estimation, ensuring that it reflects prediction reliability rather than serving as an unsupervised auxiliary output. Experiments on three public benchmarks (WHU-CD, CDD, and LEVIR-CD) show that GUF-Net achieves competitive F1 scores of 93.88%, 96.55%, and 90.43%, respectively, ranking top three on CDD and WHU-CD, while its advantage is less pronounced on LEVIR-CD. Under the strongest Gaussian, salt-and-pepper, and speckle noise conditions on the WHU-CD test set, the F1 score of GUF-Net decreases by only 0.86, 3.95, and 1.29 percentage points, respectively, exhibiting the smallest degradation among the compared models. Unlike existing uncertainty-aware baselines evaluated solely on clean data, GUF-Net jointly supervises evidential pixel uncertainty and routes it through adaptive gating, enabling stable fusion under degraded imaging conditions. Ablation studies confirm that the gated fusion, uncertainty injection, and global context aggregation each contribute to the overall performance. These results suggest that explicit uncertainty modeling and adaptive fusion can improve the reliability of BCD under noisy conditions, although there remains room for further improvement in clean-set accuracy and model efficiency.

Abstract (英文): Remote sensing semantic and referring segmentation share mask geometry but differ in whether a category vocabulary or a natural-language expression selects the output. UniRS-Seg bridges these interfaces through a shared sparse–dense query compiler and a hierarchical mask decoder, jointly trained on independently annotated datasets. Class names and referring expressions are projected from SigLIP 2 into the same decoder space; dense image–query activations supply spatial support, and sparse query tokens specify the requested region. An adapted SAM ViT-B pathway and parallel convolutional features recover local geometry, while learnable soft mask cross-attention refines both tasks and class-balanced mask-guided self-attention supports semantic parsing. UniRS-Seg achieves 57.30%, 85.31%, and 88.24% mIoU on LoveDA, Vaihingen, and Potsdam, and 78.18% and 70.88% gIoU on RefSegRS and RRSIS-D. These scores exceed the strongest listed baseline means by 0.68, 0.99, 1.18, 0.79, and 1.71 percentage points, respectively. Against separately trained semantic-only and referring-only versions of the shared architecture, joint training improves the four evaluated datasets by 0.45, 0.89, 1.40, and 0.97 points. An additional supervised RISBench evaluation achieves 72.91% gIoU and 74.85% cIoU, with the highest gIoU among the listed methods. On RefSegRS, adding learnable mask cross-attention to combined sparse and dense prompts increases gIoU by 0.86 points. The results support supervised dual-task transfer through a common query-conditioned decoder; the comparison uses published baseline results and does not establish unseen-domain or zero-shot generalization.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Sparse very-low-frequency/low-frequency (VLF/LF) lightning location is a ground-based passive remote-sensing technique, but sparse station geometry and finite-conductivity propagation can shift peak arrival times, alter peak amplitudes, and produce spurious solutions. This study proposes a three-station method integrating propagation correction with accelerating charge method (ACM)-based peak-electric-field consistency. Based on the Liénard–Wiechert field, the ACM relates the radiation field to charge acceleration and assumes a common source-side peak for the same return stroke. For given ground conductivity, relative permittivity, and an ACM reference field, the range-dependent additional delay τE corrects TDOA to obtain candidate locations. After candidate coordinates are fixed, the peak-retention factor AE, candidate-to-station distances, and local response Ki recover equivalent source-side peaks, while Jamp discriminates between correct and spurious candidates. In simulations, the in-network location error decreased from 46.0 to 21.8 m and the out-of-network error from 1867.3 to 394.8 m; the ACM-based criterion selected the correct candidate in both cases. For the S7–S8–S9 rocket-triggered-lightning case, the near-candidate error decreased from 1.333 to 0.360 km, and Jamp values of 0.0419 and 0.2238 distinguished the correct and spurious solutions. The method therefore improves sparse VLF/LF lightning location accuracy through propagation-time correction and supports spurious-solution discrimination through propagation-corrected peak-field consistency.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Total suspended solids (TSS) are a key indicator of coastal water quality and sediment dynamics, yet their long-term spatiotemporal variability remains insufficiently characterized at large spatial scales. In this study, a decade-long (2016–2025) TSS dataset for China’s coastal waters was reconstructed from Sentinel-2 multispectral imagery using a spatiotemporal graph neural network (STGNN-TSS), which integrates graph attention networks with temporal sequence modeling. The model was trained using satellite-derived reference TSS observations and evaluated using spatially separated test samples and comparative experiments. During relatively stable seasonal conditions, STGNN-TSS achieved an R2 of 0.739, an RMSE of 3.924 mg L−1, and an MAE of 1.907 mg L−1 across China’s coastal waters. The reconstructed products for other years showed consistent spatial patterns with independent satellite-derived SPM products, with Spearman correlation coefficients ranging from 0.738 to 0.891. Based on the reconstructed dataset, TSS exhibited a persistent “high-inshore, low-offshore” spatial pattern with pronounced regional differences among northern shallow seas, major estuarine systems, and southern coastal waters. Seasonal variations were identified, characterized by elevated TSS concentrations in winter and reduced concentrations in summer. Interannual variability during 2016–2025 showed an increasing trend before 2020 followed by a general decline, with substantial regional differences in variability intensity. The reconstructed TSS dataset provides a consistent basis for investigating long-term suspended sediment dynamics and supports large-scale coastal environmental monitoring and management.

Abstract (英文): Extreme precipitation and hail events over complex terrain are strongly dependent on the multiscale evolution of convective systems, yet their development remains challenging to observe and model. In this study, we apply a self-supervised learning (SSL) framework to Meteosat infrared 10.8 μm imagery over the Alpine region to derive physically meaningful representations of convective cloud organization across the full daily cycle. The learned representation organizes convective cloud scenes into three primary regimes: early convection (EC), deep convection (DC), and overcast anvil systems (OA), each exhibiting distinct cloud-top properties, spatial structures, and day–night distributions consistent with established convective development stages. Within the developed SSL framework, reported extreme precipitation and hail events are preferentially associated with these regimes, occupying the upper tail of cloud-top height, optical thickness, and cloud coverage distributions within the latent space. This indicates that extremes correspond to rare, intense realizations of continuous convective variability. Two dominant pathways of cloud structure evolution are identified: a rapid EC→DC transition and a persistent DC mode. The former is characterized by fast cloud growth and intensification, while the latter corresponds to mature convective systems with sustained cloud properties. Overall, the results demonstrate that infrared-based SSL representations capture physically consistent convective stages and their transitions, providing a novel observational framework for characterizing extreme convective events and supporting the evaluation of convection-resolving numerical weather prediction models.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate forecasting of global ionospheric total electron content (TEC) is important for space weather research and Global Navigation Satellite System (GNSS) applications. To address the complex spatiotemporal variability of TEC and the artificial discontinuity across the ±180° longitude boundary in global TEC maps, this study proposes an improved SwinLSTM model (iSwinLSTM) and further develops the ED-iSwinLSTM-ND framework for global TEC forecasting. By redesigning the shifted-window mechanism to account for longitudinal periodicity, iSwinLSTM enhances cross-boundary information interaction, while an Encoder–Decoder architecture combined with the Next-Day (ND) strategy enables prediction of the next 12 TEC maps from the preceding 36 maps. The model was evaluated retrospectively using archived GIMs from the selected test years. In 2025, a high solar activity year, the model achieved an MAE of 3.51 TECU, an RMSE of 5.10 TECU, and an R2 of 0.94; in 2020, a low solar activity year, the corresponding values were 0.96 TECU, 1.50 TECU, and 0.95. Boundary experiments further confirmed that the periodic shifted-window mechanism effectively reduces longitude-boundary errors. Overall, ED-iSwinLSTM-ND provides an effective framework for short-term global TEC forecasting by jointly exploiting spatiotemporal dependencies, longitudinal periodicity, and diurnal characteristics.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Remote sensing binary change detection aims to identify real changes in bi-temporal images while suppressing pseudo-change responses induced by complex backgrounds and temporal variations in otherwise unchanged regions. Although existing state-space-based methods incorporate cross-temporal interaction, integrating explicit difference priors into state-space parameter generation and subsequent boundary-aware decoding remains an important design problem. To address this problem, CA-Mamba, a change-aware Mamba decoding framework, is proposed. An Asymmetric Change-Aware State-Space Mixer (ACSSM) is introduced to condition state-space parameter generation on bi-temporal difference priors and to modulate the two temporal branches asymmetrically. The Cascaded Difference Prior Module (CDPM), the Change Boundary Enhancement (CBE) module, and the Change Representation Constructor (CRC) are further integrated into a unified pipeline for difference-prior generation and propagation, change-aware mixing, boundary enhancement, and region–boundary collaborative representation construction. Experiments on four public datasets, namely, LEVIR-CD, WHU-CD, CDD, and CLCD, show competitive detection performance. Ablation results support the use of signed-difference pathways and the overall CRC design under the evaluated settings. The efficiency analysis characterizes the trade-offs among detection accuracy, reported computational cost, inference latency, and parameter storage.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Robust bridge detection in ultra-high-resolution remote sensing imagery is important for transportation infrastructure mapping, geographic information system updating, and post-disaster assessment. However, local cropping can interrupt the spatial continuity of long-span bridges, leading to fragmented detections and geometrically inconsistent predictions. This paper proposes a polygon sequence network (PSeq-Net), an end-to-end sparse-global sequence modeling framework for bridge detection in very-high-resolution (VHR) remote sensing imagery. PSeq-Net generates an ordered quadrilateral vertex sequence for each bridge, unifying visual encoding and structured geometric prediction. Hierarchical sparse feature sampling preserves fine-grained evidence in bridge-relevant regions while using compact representations of background areas, enabling efficient processing of 2048 × 2048 inputs. Global context aggregation integrates long-range spatial dependencies, and geometry-aware optimization improves the structural plausibility of predicted bridge shapes. Experiments on the large-scale GLH-Bridge benchmark show that PSeq-Net achieves 37.87% average precision (AP), 73.58% AP50, and 31.38% AP75, outperforming representative oriented and large-scale bridge detectors. Further analyses highlight improved localization of elongated and boundary-crossing bridges, reduced fragmentation, and robustness across diverse scene backgrounds and image resolutions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Rapid urban growth has transformed the three-dimensional structure and land cover composition of Chinese cities, yet long-term local climate zone (LCZ) changes remain insufficiently resolved at fine spatial scales. We mapped all 17 standard LCZ classes for 105 major Chinese cities in 2000, 2005, 2010, 2015, 2020, and 2025 at a 30 m resolution. A unified random forest framework implemented on Google Earth Engine integrated Landsat imagery with spectral, topographic, building, vegetation, and ancillary land cover information. Overall accuracy ranged from 83.88% to 88.71% across the six mapping years. The mapped urban area increased from 2.14 × 104 to 5.86 × 104 km2, with the strongest relative growth shifting from eastern toward central and western China. Although the built-class area more than doubled, its share declined from 68.61% to 53.27%, while the proportion of vegetated-class area increased from 20.47% to 39.51%. LCZ D, LCZ 9, and LCZ A contributed most to the overall increase, whereas LCZ 8 declined in proportional importance. All moisture regions shifted toward more balanced built–vegetated configurations. Dryland cities, however, retained more bare surfaces and less woody vegetation than humid and transitional cities. These maps reveal both a common national trajectory and climate-conditioned regional differentiation, providing a basis for urban-climate assessment and climate-sensitive planning.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Reliable precipitation datasets are essential for basin-scale water resources assessment, climate analysis, flood and drought-related studies, whereas estimation remains challenging in arid to semi-arid, topographically complex, and data-scarce regions. This issue is particularly important in the Helmand River Basin (HRB), Afghanistan, where uncertainty in precipitation input can directly affect hydrological and hydroclimatic assessments. This study evaluated eight multi-source gridded precipitation products against gauge observations from 18 hydro-meteorological stations across the HRB. The evaluation covered daily and monthly timescales, wet and dry season conditions, rainfall-event thresholds, and elevation settings using continuous, categorical, and integrated multi-metric analyses. The results indicate that precipitation product performance in the HRB varies with temporal scale, seasonal regime, and elevation setting. In general, monthly and wet-season evaluations showed better agreement than daily and dry-season evaluations; however, this pattern reflects the combined influence of temporal scale, native product resolution, and spatial extraction method. Among the evaluated datasets, MSWEP and ERA5-Land showed the most balanced overall performance, combining better temporal agreement, lower error, lower bias, and higher rainfall-event detection across stations and elevation classes. CHIRPS also performed well, particularly at monthly and seasonal scales, whereas IMERG and PERSIANN-CDR showed moderate performance. Conversely, CMORPH and GPCC Products were the least reliable because of persistent underestimation in the former and inflated variability and overestimation in the latter. The results further indicate that dataset skill is not spatially uniform, with weaker performance generally associated with high-elevation stations. Overall, this study establishes a basin-specific framework for precipitation dataset selection in the HRB and provides a reproducible basis for selecting fit-for-purpose precipitation inputs for future water resources and hydroclimatic studies in this data-scarce region.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate precipitation estimation is crucial for water management and flood reconstructions, yet remains challenging due to sparse ground observations and systematic satellite biases. Despite the rise of deep learning in multi-source fusion, controlled comparisons under a unified framework remain scarce, and the roles of window size and precipitation product combinations remain underexplored. This study evaluated five deep learning architectures, namely 1D CNN, 2D CNN, 1D–2D CNN, 3D CNN, and Transformer, for fusing three precipitation products (IMERG, MSWEP, and ERA5-Land) to estimate station-scale daily precipitation in the Yellow River Basin, China. Results showed that deep learning models consistently outperformed individual products, weighting methods, and machine learning methods, with Transformer achieving the best overall performance, followed by 3D CNN; MSWEP led the standalone products. Spatial improvements were more pronounced in densely gauged areas, but Transformer also performed well in the sparse western regions. All products and fusion models tended to overestimate no-rain and light-rain events and underestimate moderate-to-heavy, and extreme events, with Transformer yielding the largest reductions in conditional RMSE and BIAS for heavy and extreme events. Enlarging the spatial or temporal window individually improved performance, and combining both worked even better. Ablation experiments revealed that IMERG contributed little unique information, whereas incorporating more products generally improved Transformer performance. Overall, Transformer demonstrated superior performance in fusing precipitation products and enhancing estimation accuracy.

Abstract (英文): Objects in unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) imagery are typically small, densely packed, and unevenly distributed. The prevailing focus-and-detect paradigm—first localizing focus regions where objects concentrate, and then performing detection within them—has substantially improved small object detection. Existing focusing modules, however, are almost always coupled with architectural modifications to the detector and with patch-based training, which makes the contribution of the focusing mechanism itself difficult to quantify. More importantly, whether the focusing mechanism alone can yield genuine gains on off-the-shelf detectors without any retraining remains insufficiently examined. To address these issues, we first integrate the focusing modules of several representative open-source methods into frozen detectors, removing the architectural modifications and stripping away the patch-based training on which these modules rely, so as to isolate the genuine gain of the focusing mechanism. We then establish translation equivariance as a design principle for focus-region generation and propose a strictly translation-equivariant heatmap network for this purpose. The resulting focus regions are further processed via super-resolution and image packing to construct focus images that are better suited to frozen detectors. Extensive experiments on VisDrone show that, with a strong baseline, directly plugging existing focusing modules into a frozen detector generally hurts rather than helps; when the baseline is weakened by lowering the input resolution, their gains reappear, but the final accuracy remains substantially below that of the high-resolution baseline. In contrast, our method raises the mean average precision (mAP@0.5:0.95) of multiple strong baseline detectors by 0.44–1.03 points on VisDrone-test-dev, without any retraining. When further combined with patch-based training, the overall framework achieves a total gain of 1.24–2.22 points over the strong baseline.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Ground-based hyperspectral imaging extends remote sensing to road environments by providing material-sensitive spectra for distinguishing visually similar surfaces and objects. However, pixel-wise prediction from high-dimensional spectral cubes is computationally demanding. This study presents a lightweight spectral–spatial network for efficient semantic segmentation of ground-based hyperspectral imagery. A Spectral Token Stem compresses ordered bands into pooled, normalized, and statistical descriptors before high-resolution encoding. CoordSK blocks combine coordinate-aware directional aggregation with adaptive local-scale selection, while a lightweight guided decoder recovers dense predictions from multi-level features. Experiments on the public HyKo2 and HSI-Drive datasets yield Jaccard indices of 0.6670 and 0.8181, respectively, outperforming representative efficient segmentation baselines. The model contains 0.656 million trainable parameters and reaches FP32 batch-one throughputs of 151.49 and 168.21 FPS on an RTX 5090 for the respective benchmarks. In the additional pretrained-model comparison, the network uses less than 0.5% of HSI-Adapter’s total parameters. Ablation studies validate the contributions of spectral tokenization and coordinate-selective mixing, and an input-representation study demonstrates the benefit of retaining full hyperspectral observations over PCA and pseudo-RGB inputs. These results indicate a favorable accuracy–efficiency trade-off for ground-based hyperspectral remote sensing scene understanding.

Abstract (英文): High-resolution unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) imagery is increasingly used for waterway inspection, but automated scene understanding remains constrained by the lack of pixel-level datasets that explicitly represent revetments and their complex interfaces with water, vegetation, roads, and riverside structures. RLGD contains 100 images at 3000×4000 pixels, corresponding to 1.2 billion labeled pixels. Its public RLGD taxonomy explicitly includes revetment. Seven semantic segmentation models are evaluated under a unified protocol. Across five training runs with different random seeds, mean mIoU ranges from 69.27% to 77.02%, while Hierarchical Joint Network with Boundary Guidance (GHJNet) achieves 77.02 ± 0.41% mIoU and 83.51 ± 1.18% revetment IoU. Direct transfer from Varied Drone Dataset (VDD) to RLGD results in relative mIoU reductions of 11.29–49.81%, whereas adaptation using 20% of the RLGD training source images closes 65.5% of the measured domain gap. For the GHJNet run used in the downstream analysis, object-prior refinement using the Segment Anything Model (SAM) increases revetment IoU from 84.10% to 87.25%. A 42-image visual-completeness screening case study further achieves 83.93% macro-F1 and 81.25% balanced accuracy against image-level interpretations by three experts. Overall, RLGD provides a densely annotated, task-specific benchmark for dry-season waterway semantic segmentation, revetment extraction, and image-level inspection prioritization within the surveyed Yangtze River corridor.

Abstract (英文): Growing demands for maritime security and navigational safety have made video surveillance indispensable for monitoring distant ship targets. Visible-thermal fusion provides a robust solution across varying weather and illumination conditions; however, tiny targets remain highly sensitive to positional errors, and cross-modal misalignment caused by occlusion, low illumination, and cluttered scenes further degrades detection accuracy. To address these issues, we propose CPSF-Net, a confidence-chained, position-sensitive and scale-aware fusion network for visible-thermal tiny ship detection. A Position-Sensitive Multimodal Fusion (PMF) module enhances and fuses pre-aligned visible-thermal features with possible residual local misalignment through cross-attention and derives a position-sensitive token-level matching confidence directly from the attention weights without introducing additional parameters. A Confidence-Guided Sparse Attention (CSA) module uses this confidence to adaptively suppress redundant cross-modal correlations and improve positional awareness. Finally, an adaptive Scale-Weighted Loss (SWL) balances Intersection over Union (IoU) and Normalized Wasserstein Distance (NWD) according to object scale and matching confidence, providing more stable localization supervision across object scales. On the AISail and RGBT-Tiny datasets, CPSF-Net achieves 92.1% and 84.2% mAP50 from unmanned surface vehicle (USV) and unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) perspectives, respectively. These results show that the proposed method performs consistently in challenging maritime environments.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate agricultural object extraction from high-resolution remote sensing imagery requires not only high regional segmentation accuracy but also reliable object geometry. Target-aware multiscale context strengthens target–background discrimination, contour-adaptive representation adjusts sampling to local boundary variation while preserving narrow inter-object separation, and a staged hierarchical objective extends optimization from regional agreement to curvature- and shape-related consistency. On cropland parcel and greenhouse facility datasets, ADLSC improves IoU by 8.28 and 15.92 percentage points and matching rate by 24.32 and 42.88 percentage points, respectively, relative to DeepLab V3+, while reducing curvature and shape errors. Abstract (英文): This study examines the highly variable and latitude-dependent ionospheric response to the moderate geomagnetic storm that occurred on 21–22 March 2026 over the Indian longitudinal sector using combined observations of Global Navigation Satellite System (GNSS) derived Total Electron Content (TEC) and Swarm electron density profiles. During the main phase on 21 March, pronounced positive ionospheric storm effects were observed at mid-latitudes, with Vertical TEC (VTEC) enhancements reaching up to 35 TECU, while notable negative storm effects occurred near the Equatorial Ionization Anomaly (EIA) crest region. These simultaneous observations from mid-latitude and EIA regions are attributed to the combined effects of Prompt Penetration Electric Fields (PPEFs) and the possible presence of storm-induced equatorward neutral winds. The low-latitude region exhibited moderate daytime ionospheric variations throughout the storm period, despite the presence of PPEFs. Interestingly, electron density profiles from Swarm-A and Swarm-C satellites showed a reduction around the EIA region, along with elevated electron densities near the magnetic equator during the evening period of the storm main phase. On 22 March, the storm-time ionospheric response transitioned into a more complex mixed pattern, with recovery to near-quiet levels at most stations. Moderate VTEC enhancements in the EIA region and distinct mid-latitude electron density increases are likely attributed to the thermospheric composition changes characterized by an increased O/N2 ratio. These findings highlight the synergistic roles of electrodynamic forcing, neutral wind circulation, and altered thermospheric composition in modulating storm-time ionospheric behavior. This study provides valuable insights into complex plasma dynamics at low- and mid-latitudes, with important implications for GNSS-based navigation and communication systems during geomagnetic disturbances.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV)-derived vegetation indices are increasingly used to characterize vineyard canopy variability, yet their relationships with grapevine yield and grape quality may vary between growing seasons. This two-year case study evaluated associations between multispectral vegetation indices and grapevine productivity and quality parameters in a commercial Verdelho vineyard on Madeira Island during the 2024 and 2025 growing seasons, which differed markedly in accumulated precipitation (77.3 and 249.1 mm, respectively), while thermal accumulation increased from 1411 GDD to 1525 GDD in 2025. UAV multispectral imagery was acquired at key phenological stages using a MicaSense Altum sensor. NDVI, NDRE, GNDVI, GLI, and NGRDI were evaluated in relation to yield and grape-quality parameters using Spearman rank correlations based on nine independent experimental replicates per season. To support interannual comparison despite unequal UAV survey availability, seasonal analyses were additionally based on four phenologically matched stages represented in both years. Statistical uncertainty and robustness were assessed using bootstrap confidence intervals, leave-one-out sensitivity analysis, Benjamini–Hochberg false discovery rate correction, and treatment-adjusted partial rank correlations. Relatively strong nominal associations were observed in 2024, particularly involving NDRE and GNDVI, whereas fewer nominal date-specific associations occurred in 2025. No nominal associations were detected for the phenologically matched seasonal means in 2025. However, none of the date-specific or seasonal-mean associations remained statistically significant after FDR correction. Treatment adjustment preserved the direction of all nominal associations but reduced their magnitude in most cases. The results indicate contrasting exploratory interannual patterns rather than statistically confirmed temporal instability. UAV-derived vegetation indices provided useful information on vineyard spectral variability, but relationships with yield and grape quality identified within a single season require validation across larger multi-year and multi-site datasets before broader temporal consistency or transferability can be established.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Tiny object detection remains challenging because tiny instances often occupy only a few pixels, yielding weak, sparse, and easily degraded feature responses. Feature Pyramid Networks (FPNs) are widely used to construct multi-scale feature representations, but they still face two key limitations in tiny object detection. First, conventional FPNs do not explicitly enhance weak target responses. Although existing methods leverage frequency-domain information to strengthen structural details, high-frequency responses can originate from both target boundaries and complex background textures, making indiscriminate enhancement vulnerable to background interference. Second, sparse evidence from tiny objects is highly sensitive to minor spatial deviations during cross-scale propagation and can be diluted by background-dominated feature aggregation. To address these challenges, we propose a Weak-Response-Aware Feature Pyramid Network (WRA-FPN) comprising two complementary components. The Selective Wavelet Enhancement (SWE) module uses scale-adaptive Mexican-hat wavelets to extract localized high-frequency responses and fuses them with input features and high-level semantic features to generate spatial selection weights that enhance target-relevant cues while suppressing background interference. A target–background response constraint further improves feature discriminability. The Adaptive Local–Global Alignment (ALGA) module combines continuous local cross-scale interactions with region-level global dependencies to improve spatial correspondence and preserve weak target evidence, while adaptively fusing local and global information. Extensive experiments on AI-TOD, VisDrone2019, and MS COCO 2017, using multiple representative baseline detectors including RetinaNet, Faster R-CNN, Cascade R-CNN, and DetectoRS, demonstrate improved detection performance of WRA-FPN. On AI-TOD, WRA-FPN improves AP by 4.4% and 2.4% over the corresponding RetinaNet and DetectoRS baselines, respectively.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Wavelength-conditioned remote sensing foundation models accept flexible spectral configurations, but compatibility does not ensure effective spectral utilization. We investigate this issue for hyperspectral remote sensing salient object detection and propose SpecAudit, an offline framework diagnosing input discriminability, entry-kernel geometry, and layer-wise spectral readability, together with Two-Level Spectral Adaptation (TLSA). On the 32-band hyperspectral remote sensing salient object detection dataset (HRSSD), expanding a Dynamic One-For-All (DOFA)-based pipeline from 3 to 32 bands yields no stable saliency gain. A ground-truth-assisted image-specific diagnostic readout, fitted and evaluated on spatially separated regions of each test image, yields ΔSα=+0.0418, indicating substantial image-specific spectral discriminability despite the absence of a stable direct prediction gain. TLSA combines task-shared Band Amplitude Calibration, using one learnable gain per band before the frozen entry, with Self-Guided Spectral Refinement, which fits an image-specific spectral discriminant from the coarse prediction at inference. Across five training seeds and paired image-level tests, both adaptation levels provide positive incremental improvements. TLSA increases mean Sα from 0.6472 to 0.6613, yields a positive paired improvement over the three-band reference, and exceeds the highest mean Sα among nine retrained published methods by 3.5%. These results indicate that hyperspectral gains depend on how spectral evidence is exposed and used, not only on available band count. The complete TLSA implementation and SpecAudit diagnostic code will be publicly released upon publication.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Hyperspectral image classification is an important research topic in the field of remote sensing. Its core objective is to accurately identify different land-cover categories by jointly exploiting the spatial information and spectral characteristics of pixels. However, the complex and diverse spatial structures of ground objects in hyperspectral images make it challenging for existing methods to fully capture intricate spatial patterns. To address this issue, this paper proposes a dual-branch spatial–spectral transformer with spatial feature enhancement under a spatial–spectral joint representation learning framework. Specifically, in the spatial branch, a deformable spatial feature fusion module is introduced, which combines grouped deformable convolution with Transformer to achieve fine-grained modeling of irregular spatial structures. Subsequently, a global spatial semantic aggregation module is designed to further integrate spatial features and enhance their discriminative capability. In the spectral branch, Transformer is employed to model long-range spectral dependencies, while a convolutional feed-forward network is introduced to strengthen the representation ability of critical spectral information. Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed method achieves competitive classification performance on four benchmark datasets.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate forest aboveground biomass (AGB) estimates are needed for forest inventories and carbon assessment, yet optical, synthetic aperture radar (SAR), and spaceborne lidar products describe forest properties at different spatial supports. We present LAPFuse, a multimodal framework that combines dense Sentinel-2 and Sentinel-1 observations with sparse GEDI Level 4A footprint-level aboveground biomass density records. Modality-specific encoders preserve the sensing characteristics of each input. Fixed language anchors regularize their projected representations, while distance-weighted sparse–dense attention relates GEDI footprint tokens to spatially distributed Sentinel features. A physics-informed neural process then produces a predictive mean and interval using an allometry-based soft constraint. We evaluate LAPFuse with 1847 permanent plots from the 2022–2024 growing seasons in three forest regions of China. On the held-out set of 369 plots, LAPFuse achieves R2=0.882, RMSE =15.34 Mg ha−1, and MAE =9.87 Mg ha−1. Its 95% prediction intervals attain a coverage probability of 0.949 with a mean width of 67.2 Mg ha−1. Sequential ablation shows progressive improvements as the alignment, attention, probabilistic, and allometric components are introduced. Zero-shot cross-region R2 ranges from 0.762 to 0.798, and adaptation with 100 target-region plots reduces the performance gap to 0.1–1.5% relative to in-domain performance. These results demonstrate the value of support-aware multimodal fusion for plot-level AGB estimation under this sensor configuration and these regional conditions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Ship detection in range-compressed (RC) synthetic aperture radar (SAR) images requires recognizing spatially extended responses rather than compact target appearances. Because elongated responses are not unique to ships, clutter can interfere with both feature aggregation and candidate selection. We propose FM-DETR, a frequency–morphology collaborative Transformer that extends RC-aware feature modeling to decoder-query initialization. An asymmetric cross-scale feature pyramid uses orthogonal strip convolutions to accommodate the unequal range and azimuth extents of target responses. A Fourier-gated intra-scale interaction module complements spatial self-attention with learnable spectral modulation, while a content-dependent gate regulates the spectral residual. The refined representation provides spatial guidance for multiscale candidate scoring and query selection. Evaluation follows the official RCShip-1.0 split, with synthetic training samples and scene-derived test samples reconstructed in the RC domain. FM-DETR achieves average precision (AP@0.5) of 90.14%, a precision of 89.53%, and a recall of 84.42%. With the backbone and training settings held fixed, the complete model improves AP@0.5 by 4.00 percentage points over the baseline. These results support integrating direction-sensitive aggregation, spectral refinement, and guided query initialization for ship detection in RC images.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Forest canopy height (FCH) is a key parameter for estimating forest aboveground biomass (AGB) and carbon storage. The integration of GEDI relative height products and single-angle imagery has been widely applied in FCH estimation. However, GEDI geolocation errors and the canopy underestimation of traditional single-angle imagery in dense forests limit FCH estimation accuracy. To mitigate these limitations, this study proposes an FCH estimation method that integrates GaoFen-7 (GF-7) stereo pairs, GEDI ground elevation, and Terrestrial Ecosystem Carbon Inventory Satellite (TECIS) multi-angle data. First, this study integrated GF-7 stereo pairs and GEDI elevation to generate a discrete Canopy Height Model (CHM) dataset, providing reliable training samples for FCH estimation. Secondly, to mitigate the canopy underestimation issues associated with traditional single-angle features in FCH retrieval, we further introduced TECIS multi-angle data. To fully exploit its potential for structural characterization, we combined TECIS multi-angle features and topographic features with the discrete CHM dataset, utilizing a Bayesian-optimized LightGBM model to estimate FCH. The results show that: (1) The discrete CHM dataset constructed in this study (R2 = 0.65, RMSE = 2.39 m) outperformed the GEDI RH95 product without geolocation correction (R2 = 0.37, RMSE = 3.24 m). (2) After incorporating TECIS multi-angle features into the FCH inversion model, the predictive accuracy was improved, with the coefficient of determination R2 increasing from 0.36 to 0.51, and the RMSE and MAE decreasing by 0.33 m and 0.31 m. Meanwhile, the mean bias (MB) of trees above 15 m decreased from −2.12 m to −1.49 m, and the relative bias (rBias) decreased from −12.41% to −8.78%. (3) Independent validations of the estimation results using ALS-derived CHM (R2 = 0.46, RMSE = 2.40 m) and field measurements (R2 = 0.50, RMSE = 2.95 m) confirmed the overall predictive capability of the model.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Remote sensing provides an effective means of estimating forest aboveground biomass (AGB) over large areas. However, empirical models calibrated in a specific region may lose predictive accuracy when transferred elsewhere because predictor–AGB relationships can vary with forest structure and environmental conditions. In addition, the complex nonlinear relationships learned by many machine learning models are not explicitly linked to ecological processes, limiting their ecological interpretability. To address these challenges, we developed a height-based allometric framework that incorporates the kernel normalized difference vegetation index (kNDVI) derived from Sentinel-2 imagery and leaf area index (LAI) indirectly estimated from UAV-LiDAR point clouds into the scaling relationship between mean stand height and AGB. By allowing the scaling exponent to vary with canopy spectral and structural conditions, the proposed framework was designed to account for spatial variation in the height–biomass relationship while retaining an explicit allometric structure. Its performance was compared with four commonly used machine learning methods: k-nearest neighbors (kNN), random forest (RF), extreme gradient boosting (XGBoost), and Bayesian Regularized Neural Network (BRNN). Within the calibration region, the proposed allometric model achieved an R2 of 0.67, an RMSE of 17.74 Mg ha−1, and an rRMSE of 15.17%, closely approaching the performance of RF, which was the most accurate machine learning model (R2 = 0.69, RMSE = 17.20 Mg ha−1, and rRMSE = 14.71%). More pronounced differences emerged when the models were evaluated using independent samples located outside the calibration area. Under this spatial transfer test, the allometric model retained an R2 of 0.48, with an RMSE of 22.31 Mg ha−1 and an rRMSE of 20.83%. In comparison, BRNN was the best-performing machine learning method in the same external validation but achieved an R2 of 0.32, an RMSE of 25.44 Mg ha−1, and an rRMSE of 23.75%. Introducing either LAI or kNDVI into the scaling exponent provided greater explanatory value than using a fixed exponent, while the kNDVI-based formulation (R2 = 0.41, RMSE = 23.60 Mg ha−1, and rRMSE = 22.03%) exhibited stronger spatial transferability than its LAI-based counterpart (R2 = 0.37, RMSE = 24.42 Mg ha−1, and rRMSE = 22.80%). Although all evaluated models experienced accuracy losses outside the calibration region, the reduction was substantially greater for the machine learning approaches. The proposed framework therefore provides predictive accuracy comparable to that of machine learning models within the calibration area while offering greater ecological interpretability and stronger performance under spatial transfer. This approach provides a basis for large-area forest AGB estimation by integrating optical observations with canopy structure information and could potentially be extended to applications combining optical imagery with structural information derived from satellite LiDAR. Future research could incorporate spatial autocorrelation and spatial heterogeneity into the proposed framework to further reduce uncertainty in forest AGB estimation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate wind speed nowcasting is critical for wind-risk assessment, traffic management, and emergency response. However, existing deep learning methods still suffer from inadequate multi-source meteorological data fusion, multi-step error accumulation, and performance decay at high-wind-speed thresholds. To address these issues, this paper proposes STGAF-Net, a multi-source spatiotemporal gated adaptive fusion network. It uses radar reflectivity and surface wind speed as complementary inputs, employs collaborative local–global encoding to capture local details and long-range spatial dependencies, and adaptively integrates multi-source features through stage-wise gating. Temporally, non-recurrent spatiotemporal modeling dynamically adjusts the contribution of each historical time step based on sequence evolution and time intervals, enabling parallel prediction of future wind fields. This end-to-end framework effectively bolsters spatial structure preservation and long-range dependency modeling for severe winds. The experimental results demonstrate that STGAF-Net achieves the strongest overall performance among seven learning-based baselines and a non-learning persistence forecast on two datasets, particularly at high-wind-speed thresholds and at later lead times within the 2-h nowcasting horizon.

Abstract (英文): Forest above-ground biomass (AGB) is essential for understanding forest carbon stocks and ecosystem dynamics. Despite the strong potential of synthetic aperture radar (SAR) for forest monitoring, retrieving AGB from SAR observations remains difficult because the relationship between SAR scattering responses and forest biomass is highly nonlinear, spatially variable, and often difficult to represent with both accuracy and interpretability. To address this issue, this study proposes PhySR-Bio, a multi-stage physics-aware symbolic regression framework for interpretable SAR-based forest biomass retrieval. The physics-aware design is achieved by incorporating SAR scattering-related variables, incidence angle information, and physically meaningful feature combinations into symbolic regression. Unlike black box regression models, PhySR-Bio derives an analytical formula in which the selected variables and their mathematical relationships can be directly inspected. On the independent test set, PhySR-Bio achieved an RMSE of 45.083 Mg/ha, an MAE of 33.997 Mg/ha, and an R of 0.527. It reduced RMSE and MAE by up to 6.35% and 8.57%, respectively, compared with representative semi-empirical formulas, and achieved a test RMSE within 2.3% of the best-performing black box machine learning model while retaining an explicit and interpretable expression.

Abstract (英文): The quality of the SWOT-derived marine gravity field and its subsequent refinement are investigated in the Western Pacific (120–140°E, 0–30°N). Inverting 41 cycles of the SWOT L3_LR_SSH v2.0.1 product yields a gravity field with RMSEs of 2.476 and 4.321 mGal against independent JAMSTEC and NCEI shipborne gravity, respectively, and the accuracy gain converges as the number of SWOT altimetry repeat cycles increases, showing diminishing marginal returns. To further refine this field, three tree-based ensemble learning methods—Random Forest (RF), histogram-based gradient boosting (HGB) and LightGBM (LGBM)—are trained on 67 multi-source features with the SWOT-minus-shipborne residual as the target, and validated under two spatially isolated strategies: a 2° × 2° spatial block hold-out with a 10 km buffer and a cruise-wise hold-out over seven complete cruises, in which training samples within 10 km of any test point are removed. All three methods reduce the error. On the block hold-out, LGBM performs best (RMSE 2.398 mGal, a 3.6% improvement over the 2.488 mGal baseline), followed by HGB (2.402 mGal, 3.5%) and RF (2.422 mGal, 2.7%); on the cruise hold-out the gains are markedly larger, with HGB best (RMSE 1.703 mGal, a 8.6% improvement over the 1.863 mGal baseline), then LGBM (1.714 mGal, 8.0%) and RF (1.794 mGal, 3.7%). A consistent reversal is found between the two assessment levels: RF leads on the in-training out-of-fold RMSE but is weakest on unseen cruises, indicating that RF is competitive within the training distribution yet weaker than boosting methods when extrapolating to unsurveyed areas. Abstract (英文): Dense fixed-wing UAV swarms can produce overlapping HRRPs, for which echo separation may truncate extended-target responses and propagate reconstruction errors. This paper proposes an ICWE + SPMP + FWDC framework for simultaneous multi-target ISAR imaging. ICWE extracts fixed windows under multi-target masking. SPMP jointly estimates initial range cell locations and radial motion parameters through structured trajectory matching and search-space peeling. FWDC compensates the original mixed echo for each target and coherently combines the compensated components retained by their associated windows. The framework couples target-wise motion estimation with simultaneous focusing of overlapping targets. Experiments using ideal scattering center and electromagnetic simulation data demonstrate comparable visual focusing to the comparison methods under the evaluated low-SNR conditions with HRRP overlap, with less parameter estimation time and lower compensation cost. The results support a model-driven approach to simultaneous imaging without reconstructing individual target echoes.

Abstract (英文): Archaeological stratigraphy serves as the core foundation for establishing archaeological chronological sequences, clarifying ancient human activity patterns, and revealing the evolution of human–land relationships. Current field stratigraphic delineation mainly relies on manual visual observation and empirical judgement, which suffers from strong subjectivity and poor repeatability. It is difficult to accurately identify quasi-homogeneous archaeological strata with similar paedogenic backgrounds and soil characteristics, easily resulting in stratigraphic misclassification and boundary misjudgement. Hyperspectral imaging technology can effectively capture subtle spectral differences in soil physicochemical components and support the refined identification of homogeneous strata, whereas its practical application is restricted by high data dimensionality, severe band redundancy, and insufficient targeted extractions of effective features. Taking five typical archaeological deposits, including Warring States–Han, Song–Yuan, and Ming–Qing cultural layers, ash pits, and undisturbed subsoil, this study constructs a spectral–spatial collaborative deep learning model (ABS-Net) with an adaptive band selection module based on field-measured hyperspectral data of archaeological profiles. The proposed model realises the automatic mining of subtle spectral features and intelligent stratigraphic classification. Comparative experiments with multiple state-of-the-art hyperspectral classification models demonstrate that ABS-Net achieves competitive and stable classification accuracy. This study verifies the feasibility and application potential of combining hyperspectral remote sensing with deep learning for intelligent stratigraphic classification, providing a novel technical reference for intelligent stratigraphic interpretation in field archaeology.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The required amount of water for crop production differs from region to region, mainly due to the decreasing “effectiveness” of rainfall in maintaining plant growth due to increasing evaporation. Effective rainfall is related to the available moisture in the plant’s root zone, allowing the plant to germinate, emerge, and maintain its growth. There is, thus, a need to design agrometeorological and rain gauge networks for monitoring suitable crops, leading to sustainable agriculture. However, the minimum density needed for a rain-gauge network depends on the variability in space and time of the intended precipitation fields, and a variety of factors, including topographical features, accessibility, and ease of maintenance, influence the choice of rain-gauge locations. The number of rain gauges, rather than their location, is what optimizes a rain gauge network for various time scales, such as daily or monthly data. To map mean daily or mean monthly precipitation over watersheds or medium-sized agroclimatic zones and monitor crops for sustainable agriculture, this study focuses on the number of grid cells leading to the design of a rain gauge network, specifically a regular rain gauge grid scenario, considering the areal extent of land suitable for agriculture based on the agroclimatic zoning methodology. The universal kriging approach, an interpolation method based on geostatistical analysis, is used to calculate the required number of grid cells for the network’s density. Geostatistical analysis focuses on estimating and selecting the semi-variogram (SV) to determine the range or the decorrelation distance between rain gauges for precipitation estimation, superimposed on a grid mesh that encompasses the area’s agricultural land, such as Thessaly in Greece, as delineated by the agroclimatic zoning methodology. The daily and monthly mean climatological precipitation database is used, based on the CHIRPS-v3 dataset. The results indicate that the best fit for both daily and monthly mean precipitation is provided by the Spherical SV model, and the corresponding maximum range, which also represents the decorrelation distance, is calculated at 28.162 km for both mean daily precipitation and mean monthly precipitation, respectively. In summary, the size of the selected grid cells, based on the computed range of the selected SV, is 28 × 28 km2 for the region of Thessaly, where one rain gauge is expected to be installed in each grid, leading to a total number of 18 grid cells.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Geophysical and remote sensing surveys of Pacific Northwest Coast plankhouses, sweathouses, and other architectural features have been a growing component of Pacific Northwest Coast archaeological studies. Most of these studies have focused on coastal plankhouses with associated shell middens, especially in British Columbia. Limited geophysical examples exist of Pacific Northwest Coast architectural features from the southern region, including northern California and Oregon, especially in riverine settings. In this study, we integrate ground-penetrating radar and magnetic gradiometry to document subsurface features in collaboration with the Tsnungwe Council. Our findings, based on our interpretations of the observed data but not ground-truthed at this time, document subsurface features ranging from ~2 m wide pits to large rectangular plankhouses ~6 m wide and 12 m long. These houses include central subterranean pits, which contain a hearth, that are ~5 m wide. Our findings support the interpretation of a large multicomponent village site with several plankhouses present, providing a unique example of southern Pacific Northwest Coast Indigenous architecture. Future ground-truthing efforts may confirm and/or revise these interpretations.

Abstract (英文): Oriented remote sensing object detection (ORSOD) remains challenging because objects usually appear at arbitrary rotations, with large aspect-ratio variations and dense spatial layouts. Although recent detectors have achieved notable progress, most still rely on standard convolutional backbones, whose limited inherent rotation equivariance weakens robustness to rotated targets and reduces localization reliability. To address these issues, this paper proposes RedhDet, an ORSOD framework based on rotation-equivariant feature learning and dual-head decoupling. Specifically, a Ladder-Structured Implicitly Equivariant Network (LSIEN) is developed as the backbone to extract rotation-equivariant features in a soft manner, without relying on strictly constrained kernels or equivariant pre-trained weights. In addition, a decoupled dual-head architecture is introduced, in which a fully connected branch is used for classification and a convolutional branch is used for bounding box regression, thereby better matching the distinct requirements of semantic recognition and precise localization. Experimental results on the DOTA-1.0 and HRSC2016 datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method. On the DOTA-1.0 test set, RedhDet improves mAP50 by 2.09% and mAP75 by 14.84% over the baseline. On HRSC2016, gains of 14.12% and 51.85% are achieved for mAP50 and mAP75, respectively. With multi-scale training and testing, the proposed method achieves 80.40% mAP on DOTA-1.0, indicating its effectiveness and competitiveness for ORSOD.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate and transferable flood segmentation from Earth observation (EO) imagery remains challenging because models trained on specific geographic regions and environmental conditions often exhibit limited generalization. This study proposes Global–Local Feature Context and Reconstruction (GL-FCR), a task-specific downstream decoder designed to adapt compact EO foundation-model representations for spatially detailed flood mapping. Integrated with Prithvi-EO-2.0, GL-FCR enhances the reconstruction of dense flood maps while maintaining contextual information from the pretrained representation. On the SEN1Floods11 standard test set, Prithvi + GL-FCR achieved a flood F1 score of 91.08%, compared with 90.02% on the geographically held-out Bolivia subset in the original experiment. Across three random seeds, GL-FCR achieved mean flood IoU scores of 83.07 ± 0.50% and 81.53 ± 0.27% on the standard test and Bolivia subsets, respectively, demonstrating reproducible performance across training runs. Additional SAR-only and optical–SAR experiments showed that TerraMind-B + GL-FCR achieved the strongest SAR-only performance, with F1 scores of 81.53% and 82.28% on the standard test and Bolivia subsets, respectively, whereas adding SAR to strong clear-scene optical representations provided limited additional benefit. Furthermore, zero-shot cross-dataset evaluation across all 509 WorldFloods v2 scenes yielded a flood F1 score of 84.56% and a flood IoU of 73.24% for Prithvi + GL-FCR, compared with 83.35% and 72.15%, respectively, for the Prithvi native decoder, without additional training or adaptation. Overall, the results demonstrate that task-specific global–local reconstruction can effectively leverage EO foundation-model representations for accurate and transferable flood segmentation across geographic regions and datasets.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate prediction of significant wave height (SWH) is essential for ocean engineering safety, maritime transportation, and wave energy utilization. To address the nonlinear and non-stationary characteristics of SWH time series, this paper proposes a hybrid forecasting model that integrates signal decomposition, mode grouping and reconstruction, and deep learning. First, the model employs noise-assisted multivariate empirical mode decomposition (NA-MEMD) to adaptively decompose the multivariate time series into intrinsic mode functions (IMFs) to alleviate the problem of mode mixing. Subsequently, permutation entropy is employed to assess the complexity of the mode components and guide their grouping into two branches. Finally, WaveNet and long short-term memory (LSTM) networks are used to model and forecast the relatively high-complexity (fast-varying) and low-complexity (slow-varying) components, respectively, and the final forecast is generated by superimposing the predictions of all components. The proposed model was validated using measured data from two buoy stations, and evaluated by four metrics: root mean square error (RMSE), mean absolute error (MAE), symmetric mean absolute percentage error (sMAPE), and correlation coefficient (R). Experimental results show that the proposed model achieves lower RMSE, MAE, and sMAPE, and higher R compared with the baseline models across all evaluated forecast horizons and buoy sites.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Detecting submerged objects becomes difficult when suspended particles alter spectral signatures and reduce target–background contrast. A detector trained in clear water can consequently lose discrimination and produce extensive background responses as turbidity increases. Here, we present a target-specific framework that couples progressive domain adaptation with object-level response calibration. A nonlinear unmixing autoencoder first learns the target representation from labeled clear-water observations. Cross-turbidity progressive domain adaptation (CTP-DA) then transfers model states, target prototypes, and prediction memory through ordered unlabeled domains. Risk-gated object-level background suppression (RG-OBS) subsequently limits background activation while protecting supported target regions. Across the L1–L5 target domains of the controlled brick-and-stone benchmark, the framework achieved an average precision of 0.9282. This exceeded the strongest external comparator evaluated here by 7.10 percentage points. Ablation experiments linked the ranking improvement to progressive adaptation and the reduction in background response to subsequent spatial calibration. Together, these findings support combining intermediate unlabeled domains with spatial target evidence to sustain underwater detection across controlled turbidity changes.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate spatial mapping of apple orchards is essential for regional agricultural planning and sustainable management; however, large-scale, multi-year mapping remains challenging because spectral confusion and climate-driven inter-annual phenological variation complicate interpretation. To address these challenges, we propose a Phenological Synchronization and Time–Frequency Feature Selection framework (FSITF) for high-precision apple orchard identification using Sentinel-2 time-series observations. We evaluated the framework in Shandong and Shaanxi provinces, two major apple-producing regions in China. FSITF consists of three main components: phenological synchronization, time–frequency feature construction, and discriminative feature selection. First, we develop a phenological synchronization strategy based on derivative analysis and correlation-based temporal alignment to reduce inter-annual and regional temporal discrepancies in phenological trajectories, thereby generating standardized cross-year phenological trajectories. Second, Harmonic Analysis is combined with temporal phenological metrics to construct a complementary time–frequency feature representation, enabling the characterization of complex perennial growth dynamics from both temporal evolution and periodic variation perspectives. Third, a dual-metric feature selection strategy integrating Jeffries–Matusita distance-based class separability and Random Forest-based feature importance is developed to identify informative features and reduce high-dimensional redundancy. Experimental results demonstrate that FSITF achieves overall accuracies exceeding 93% and Kappa coefficients above 0.85 in both study regions, outperforming conventional approaches without phenological synchronization and feature optimization. These results indicate that integrating phenological alignment, time–frequency feature representation, and orchard-oriented feature selection can effectively improve the accuracy and robustness of regional perennial crop mapping, providing a reliable framework for large-scale orchard monitoring.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Typhoon wind prediction is important for disaster prevention. ERA5 reanalysis provides spatially continuous wind fields but may smooth local structures, whereas FY-3E WindRAD, a dual-frequency scatterometer, observes ocean surface winds through C-band and Ku-band measurements with sparse coverage and temporal mismatch. This study evaluates the conditional value of fusing FY-3E WindRAD data with ERA5 for 12-h short-term typhoon wind vector prediction using matched 2023 samples. We compared ERA5-only, naive, mask-aware, and gated fusion across coverage-sensitivity, band-comparison, and temporal-window experiments. Results show that FY-3E fusion provides conditional numerical benefits rather than consistent improvements. Under the band-specific evaluation, Ku-band gated fusion reduced vector root mean square error (RMSE) from 5.736 to 5.366 m/s and average wind speed error (AWSE) from 3.597 to 3.276 m/s, although these improvements were not statistically significant after multiple-comparison correction. Dual-band gated fusion did not outperform the ERA5-only baseline, and under the common-sample evaluation neither Ku-band nor Dual-band showed a clear advantage. The ±3 h window provided a more favorable balance between sample availability and temporal consistency than the wider ±6 h window. Overall, FY-3E WindRAD data provides complementary information, but its effectiveness depends on coverage, band selection, temporal matching, and fusion strategy.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Solar-induced chlorophyll fluorescence (SIF) is a powerful proxy for photosynthesis, yet its quantitative relationship with gross primary productivity (GPP) varies considerably among ecosystems. The underlying mechanisms for this variability, particularly in structurally complex forests, remain poorly understood. Using tower-based SIF and eddy-covariance measurements from two contrasting plantation forests—a drought-tolerant Quercus variabilis and a fast-growing poplar—we investigated how species-specific light-energy allocation strategies influence SIF-GPP relationships and the performance of mechanistic light-reaction models. The key findings are as follows: (1) The photochemical mechanistic model substantially reduced the underestimation of GPP under high-light conditions. A simplified qL-PAR model, derived from leaf-level active fluorescence measurements, produced comparable performance to that of the full photochemical model, i.e., the MLR-NPQ method—a mechanistic light-response model with canopy SIF. (2) The MLR model captured seasonal light-energy partitioning dynamics across two plantations and revealed contrasting light-energy allocation patterns between the two plantation systems. Poplar prioritized photochemical efficiency (ΦP, averaging 0.41) to maximize carbon gain, whereas Q. variabilis invested more energy in NPQ and SIF for a photoprotective “safety-first” strategy. (3) However, MLR models exhibited inconsistent performance. For the poplar plantation, the model achieved higher accuracy (R2 = 0.83 at the daily scale and R2 = 0.65 at the half-hourly scale) than for the Q. variabilis plantation (R2 = 0.65 at the daily scale and R2 = 0.45 at the half-hourly scale). (4) The contrasting ΦP–ΦF relationships provide a physiological explanation for the species-specific performance of the SIF-based GPP models. For poplar, ΦP–ΦF maintained a tight linear relationship across a wide range of moderate-to-high PAR levels. In contrast, for Q. variabilis under high PAR (ΦP = 0.249), NPQ became saturated while SIF continued to rise, leading to a decoupling of the ΦP–ΦF relationship. Our results suggest that the contrasting light-energy allocation patterns observed at the two sites may contribute to the performance discrepancies in canopy-scale SIF-based GPP simulations. Our results collectively validate that a photochemical mechanistic framework advances the process-oriented simulation of photosynthetic energy partitioning in plantation canopies. These findings highlight the potential need to integrate species-specific ΦP–ΦF relationships to better constrain the competitive balance between carbon gain and photoprotective safety—a key uncertainty that must be reconciled for robust GPP retrievals over managed forests but requires further validation. These findings contribute to a mechanistic understanding of SIF–GPP relationships in tall forests and provide a reference for improving vegetation parameterization in regional-scale modeling applications where such ecosystems are represented.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Floods, earthquakes, and landslides may require a multi-satellite synthetic aperture radar (SAR) system to insert new observations after a regular plan has been prepared but before upload. The revised plan must respect visibility, imaging chronology, look-direction switching, orbit resources, strip formation, and plan stability. We propose a multi-relational graph convolutional network with constrained proximal policy optimization (GCN-PPO). Five opportunity relations encode conflict, feasible sequence, cross-satellite competition, demand alternatives, and strip synergy. A three-level policy selects the action category, logical completion unit, and observation opportunity, while feasibility-normalized constraints regulate emergency-service shortfall and plan perturbation. In a 300 s equal-budget experiment, all 750 returned schedules were feasible and free of hard-constraint violations, and 96.4% passed the preregistered timing contract. Under natural termination, GCN-PPO responded in 102.4 s on average, 9.35% of the ALNS time, and met the normalized emergency target in 98.1% of evaluations. Its per-edit perturbation was 31.4% lower than MLP-PPO and 35.9% lower than ALNS. Timely executable schedules support rapid SAR data acquisition for downstream disaster-object detection and emergency monitoring.

</details>

Abstract (英文): L-band Vegetation Optical Depth (VOD) retrievals in tropical forest almost always use the τ–ω model, which parameterizes canopy scattering with an effective single-scattering albedo and usually neglects higher-order scattering. This work reports nine months of continuous observations from a four-channel ground-based GNSS-Transmissometry/Reflectometry (GNSS-T/GNSS-R) instrument on the 45 m walk-up tower at the LBA K67 site, Tapajós National Forest, Brazil. Four antennas feed two dual-channel receivers for the direct, reflected (LHCP and RHCP), and canopy-transmitted (RHCP) signals. Three direct observational diagnostics are consistent with a canopy volume-scattering contribution. The transmitted opacity increases with incidence angle but recovers only part of the expected path-length dependence, shows no dependence on the LiDAR-derived canopy height, and the circular polarization ratio departs from the angular decline of a coherent reflection. A joint retrieval recovers the apparent volume-scattering amplitude Gvω0 as a positive residual of the measured return over the modeled coherent two-way ground path. Gvω0 is an effective scattering residual, not the τ–ω single-scattering albedo ω. For GPS L1 it decreases from 0.85[0.59,1.19] to 0.41[0.28,0.57] through the dry-to-wet transition. This seasonal magnitude is an internal-consistency result from the measurement chain, without independent validation. It depends on two conservative choices. A smooth-surface soil model maximizes the subtracted coherent ground return. A two-term decomposition omits the canopy–ground interaction paths.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Ground-based Global Navigation Satellite System (GNSS) vertical displacements provide an independent measure of hydrological loading, but their ability to resolve terrestrial water storage (TWS) variations in arid inland basins remains uncertain. This study compared vertical displacements from 15 GNSS stations within and around the Heihe River Basin with loading displacements derived from satellite-based GRACE and model-based Noah TWS anomalies. After removing non-hydrological signals from GNSS series, correlations among the network-mean vertical displacement for GNSS-GRACE, GNSS-Noah, and GRACE-Noah were 0.48, 0.49, and 0.74, respectively. Mean station-level correlations for the same comparisons were lower (0.38, 0.35, and 0.60, respectively), with weaker agreement within the basin interior. Time-series decomposition showed that GNSS captured inter-annual TWS variability more consistently than the seasonal cycle of TWS. GNSS generally peaks earlier than GRACE in the south but later in the north. Noah interannual variations diverged from both GNSS and GRACE, suggesting limitations in the model’s representation of arid-region hydrological processes. Overall, GNSS provides an independent constraint on long-term TWS variability and a diagnostic of land surface model performance. These findings support multisource monitoring of arid-basin water storage while highlighting the need for improved scale matching and in situ validation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The future trajectory of tropical vegetation greening remains highly controversial, and the dry-season dynamics of tropical rainforests represent a critical knowledge gap. To advance understanding, this study integrates a suite of multisource satellite vegetation indexes (VIs) and process-based ecosystem models, aiming to comprehensively assess long-term trends in both canopy greenness and photosynthetic capacity during the dry season, and to critically evaluate the inter-product discrepancies in trend detection. Furthermore, we applied partial least squares regression (PLS) to isolate the relative contributions of major climatic factors to dry-season vegetation change. Results showed that great discrepancies in trends of vegetation greenness and productivity from different VIs, presenting that, dry-season canopy greenness (leaf area index, normalized difference vegetation index), and vegetation photosynthesis (sun-induced chlorophyll fluorescence), consistently displayed an increasing trend in tropical rainforest areas during the dry season over the past four decades at the average rate of 5.0 × 10−3 m3·m−3·yr−1, 0.3 × 10−3 yr−1, and 0.9 × 10−3 W·m−2·nm−1·sr−1·yr−1, however, vegetation productivity (gross primary productivity, and vegetation optical depth with C, X, and Ku bands) showed large decreasing trends at a rate of −5.0 × 10−3 gC·m−2·yr−1, −0.7, −0.1, and −0.7 × 10−3 yr−1, respectively, and it was occupied with great spatial disparities that the initial increasing GPP in Congo weakened over time, shifting around the early 2000s from a positive trend (1982–2002) to a negative trend thereafter (2002–2018), while this trend was overwhelmingly contrary to GPP in Southeast Asia rainforest. Multisource remote-sensed VIs did not consistently support a significant greening tendency across the tropical rainforests during the dry season. However, process-based models consistently predicted a robust increasing trend of LAI and GPP in the dry-season tropical rainforests throughout the 21st century at the rate of 0.9 × 10−3 m3·m−3·yr−1 and 2.4 × 10−3 gC·m−2·yr−1. CO2, vapor pressure deficit (VPD), temperature, and solar radiation, defined as the heat-related variables, played a largely negative role in contributing to vegetation growth in dry-season tropical rainforests from different VIs, and the water-related factors, including PRE, SM, and ET, positively contributed to vegetation growth. In terms of contributions, rising CO2 concentration, temperature, and VPD were detected as three of the most important drivers of changes in tropical rainforests, with a contribution degree of 45%, 25%, and 20%, respectively. This study emphasizes that a comprehensive evaluation framework for data assimilation between multisource remote-sensed data and process-based model data in the tropics, which helps reduce uncertainties induced by intensifying climate change and the absence of observation stations, needs to be further improved and refined.

</details>

Abstract (英文): A dataset of uncompressed synthetic aperture radar (SAR) raw data has been acquired with Sentinel-1C and Sentinel-1D, providing, for the first time, complex raw data representative of the instrument signal prior to lossy onboard compression. Comprised of 65 Level-0 products and made openly available to the community, it enables a direct assessment of onboard processing algorithms with measurements unaffected by the flexible dynamic block adaptive quantization (FDBAQ) nominally applied by Sentinel-1. A detailed description of the data is presented together with an analysis of the achieved Sentinel-1 FDBAQ compression performance, obtained by emulating the compression/decompression process. The impact of the FDBAQ quantization on SAR radiometry and image quality is analyzed in both the single-look complex (SLC) and raw data domains. The released dataset and derived metrics are intended to establish a quantitative reference for evaluating future SAR compression schemes against the nominal Sentinel-1 FDBAQ implementation, supporting the development of onboard processing and compression-aware machine-learning algorithms.
