Abstract (英文): Unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) scanning and mobile laser scanning provide detailed three-dimensional observations for forest inventory and structural monitoring for which point-wise separation of ground, understory, woody components, and foliage is fundamental. Modern segmentation pipelines can produce multiple predictions from different feature configurations, training objectives, random seeds, or network architectures. However, equal and fixed-weight fusion implicitly assume stable source quality, although the relative reliability of these predictions can vary across semantic classes and sensing conditions, leading to error propagation and negative transfer. We therefore formulate multi-source prediction integration as a calibration-supported class-wise reliability decision problem and propose the unified class-wise reliability gate (UCRG). The UCRG measures global and class-wise source reliability from labeled calibration data, uses shrinkage to stabilize class-specific estimates when labels are scarce, and conditionally performs soft fusion or hard source selection according to the reliability gap. An optional calibration-guided confusion-pair refiner further targets residual ambiguity between semantically similar classes. All the reliability estimation and parameter selection processes are performed without query labels. Experiments on FOR-instance, EvoMLS, and Lin3D cover UAV and mobile laser scanning together with cross-dataset transfer. Using one shared parameterization across the three primary protocols, the UCRG obtains 70.68% mIoU and records two negative-transfer episodes across the six evaluated episodes compared with six for fixed-weight fusion. When the gate parameters are selected solely from the available calibration support in each deployment, the UCRG reaches 59.20% mean mIoU in the PTv3-augmented Lin3D candidate pool. These results show that reliability-guided decisions can exploit class-dependent complementarity across heterogeneous frozen predictors and sensing conditions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Urban trees provide important ecosystem services; however, municipal inventories used to characterize urban-forest composition remain incomplete and inconsistent among cities. We developed a multimodal deep-learning framework that combined municipal inventories with tree-centered National Agriculture Imagery Program aerial imagery, airborne LiDAR canopy height, Sentinel-2 time series and phenology, Google Satellite Embeddings, and daily weather. Beginning with 5.43 million records from 60 U.S. cities, quality-control and data-availability screening retained 1.85 million records representing 579 taxa across 36 cities. Because many species and genera had overlapping remote-sensing signatures, a supervised discrimination model identified 11 intermediate groups that were subsequently collapsed into six remote-sensing-derived taxonomic groups. On an independent test set of 161,508 records, the final pruned ensemble achieved 0.793 accuracy, 0.772 macro-F1, and 0.792 weighted-F1. Group-specific F1 ranged from 0.705 to 0.867 among trees with LiDAR-derived vegetation height ≥ 2 m. Gate weights emphasized high-resolution NAIP-based inputs, whereas ablation and single-modality analyses showed that Sentinel-2 and weather time series supplied important complementary temporal information. Mean accuracy and macro-F1 declined to 0.685 and 0.478 when target cities were withheld but remained substantially higher than for models trained using individual cities. This framework supports scalable classification of individual urban trees into broader remotely distinguishable groups, not species-level identifications or ecological functional types. Sentinel-2 measurements provide contextual information at 10 m resolution rather than individual-crown observations.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Protected Areas (PAs) are essential for global biodiversity conservation, yet assessing their management and conservation effectiveness remains a formidable challenge. While remote sensing (RS) enables synoptic, large-scale monitoring, existing reviews are often fragmented—either decoupled from PA governance contexts or confined to isolated threat mappings. To bridge this gap, this review proposes a dual-dimensional analytical framework integrating management effectiveness and conservation effectiveness. Based on the literature retrieved via a systematic Boolean search in the Web of Science, we first establish a multi-source RS technology stack covering space-borne optical, radar, nighttime-light, LiDAR and cloud-computing platforms. We then review RS applications within the two dimensions. For management effectiveness, we trace the progression from single-disturbance tracking to integrated pressure assessments using counterfactual matching designs. For conservation effectiveness, we synthesize advances in biodiversity dynamics, ecosystem pattern integrity, quality evaluation, and ecosystem service quantification. Our review demonstrates that RS has become a major component of large-scale PA evaluation, revealing measurable successes while highlighting the risk of overestimating conservation outcomes when relying solely on greenness-based proxies. Persistent challenges include the lack of standardized, biome-specific assessment frameworks and the disconnect between biophysical monitoring and socio-ecological drivers. Addressing these gaps requires unified evaluation systems aligned with the Kunming-Montreal Global Biodiversity Framework, AI-driven multi-sensor fusion, and the operationalization of RS into routine PA governance to support the “30 × 30” target and ecological product value realization.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Transformer-based methods have achieved remarkable performance in hyperspectral image (HSI) classification. However, their ability to learn discriminative multi-scale spatial–spectral features remains limited by the single-granularity token-to-token interactions in conventional self-attention and the imbalanced modeling of spatial and spectral information. To address these limitations, we propose a multi-granularity weight transformer (MGWT) network comprising a spatial–spectral grouped parallel residual tokenizer (S2-GPRT) and a multi-granularity attention (MGA) module. Specifically, the S2-GPRT partitions the spectral bands into groups according to their physical characteristics and employs a dual-branch architecture to extract complementary local spatial–spectral features from individual spectral groups and the full spectrum. MGA integrates three components: a multi-granularity aggregator (Agg), a spatial–spectral dual-stream attention mechanism (S2-DSA), and a learnable mixed top-k attention mechanism (LMTA). The Agg preserves a token-level pathway while constructing group-proxy pathways with progressively larger local receptive fields, enabling attention to integrate fine-grained information and multi-scale context within a single block. The S2-DSA captures spatial and spectral dependencies through dedicated spatial and spectral attention streams. Furthermore, the LMTA constructs four rank-guided attention branches with different top-k selection ratios and adaptively fuses their outputs to integrate complementary attention responses at varying levels of sparsity. Experiments on three benchmark datasets demonstrate that the MGWT outperforms state-of-the-art methods in HSI classification.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Volumetric radar nowcasting requires models to represent storm evolution across time and altitude without incurring prohibitive computational cost. Conventional two-dimensional approaches omit explicit vertical structure, whereas many three-dimensional models rely on computationally expensive volumetric operators. Reflectivity-only forecasting further fails to exploit complementary information from auxiliary radar variables and terrain. We therefore propose TerraCast3D, an end-to-end framework for deterministic gridded volumetric radar nowcasting conditioned on three polarimetric variables, namely differential reflectivity ZDR, specific differential phase KDP, and copolar correlation coefficient ρHV, together with Doppler spectrum width σV and terrain. TerraCast3D combines source-specific altitude-grouped encoding, terrain-modulated gated fusion, terrain-conditioned modulation of low-altitude reconstruction features, and lightweight spatiotemporal modeling to predict future reflectivity volumes while limiting the use of dense 3D operations. Experiments using GridRad-Severe volumetric radar data from 2019 to 2023 show that TerraCast3D achieves the highest CSI and HSS among the evaluated 3D models at all examined reflectivity thresholds while requiring substantially fewer FLOPs. At 40 dBZ, its full-volume CSI and HSS reach 0.3044 and 0.4501, respectively. Ablation experiments show that auxiliary radar variables mainly improve full-volume continuous-field accuracy and categorical skill at 20 and 30 dBZ, whereas joint auxiliary radar and terrain conditioning yields the highest 40 dBZ and threshold-averaged categorical scores. These findings show that altitude-aware representation and adaptive multi-source conditioning can strengthen intense-echo prediction while maintaining low arithmetic complexity in volumetric radar nowcasting.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Reliable through-wall human imaging is challenging because strong wall clutter and motion-dependent target responses can degrade target preservation, particularly under low signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) conditions. This paper proposes a motion-adaptive through-wall moving-target enhancement framework based on energy-guided competitive fusion. After wall clutter suppression and wall-compensated imaging, each pixelwise complex slow-time sequence is decomposed into complementary zero-Doppler and nonzero-Doppler response branches. Kapur maximum-entropy segmentation is then used to construct a common candidate support, from which a data-derived three-branch energy reference distribution is obtained for the zero-Doppler, nonzero-Doppler, and virtual-background hypotheses. The resulting reference-weighted Softmax fusion is formulated as the closed-form solution of a Kullback–Leibler (KL)-regularized optimization problem. Simulations and measured through-wall experiments show that the two decomposed branches provide different relative responses to targets with different motion states, while the proposed fusion improves overall target-background separation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Visual detection of unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs) is an essential perception component of urban low-altitude security systems. However, UAV targets in complex urban scenes appear small and exhibit weak visual features. Existing convolutional structures inadequately extract their contours and fine-grained details, and background clutter such as building textures and trees attenuates target feature responses. To address these problems, this paper proposes a lightweight detection model, SA-YOLO (Small-target Adaptive YOLO). A bilateral detail refinement convolution (BDRConv) strengthens target details while preserving primary features through dual-branch collaboration. An adaptive SimAM (ASimAM) attention mechanism modulates attention response strength via a learnable scaling factor. DySample dynamic upsampling achieves sub-pixel-level reconstruction of cross-scale features through content-adaptive sampling offsets. A weighted joint loss based on the normalized Wasserstein distance improves bounding-box regression for small targets. On the DUT-Anti-UAV and the self-constructed C-UAV datasets, SA-YOLO achieves mAP50-95 values of 52.1% and 43.2%, respectively, with only 2.41 M parameters at 171 FPS. Across five training seeds, the mAP50-95 improvement over the baseline is statistically significant on DUT-Anti-UAV, whereas on C-UAV the gain is directionally consistent but not statistically significant. Compared with the accuracy-leading detectors, SA-YOLO delivers comparable accuracy in a more compact model, achieving a favorable accuracy–model-size tradeoff at a real-time inference speed.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Marine oil spill accidents pose serious threats to marine ecosystems and coastal economic development. X-band marine radar provides an effective means for nearshore oil spill monitoring, yet accurate oil film segmentation remains challenging because of grayscale overlap between oil films and sea clutter, range-dependent echo attenuation, and noise interference. To address these challenges, an unsupervised oil spill detection method integrating HBA-AE ROI extraction with IAEA fine segmentation is proposed. In the coarse detection stage, an autoencoder (AE) maps pixel grayscale intensity, local mean, and local standard deviation into a compact two-dimensional latent space. The AE-derived latent representation is further optimized by HBA to enhance oil film–sea clutter separability, after which K-Means clustering identifies candidate oil spill ROIs. In the subsequent fine segmentation stage, the extracted ROI constrains the search domain, while the Improved Alpha Evolution Algorithm (IAEA) maximizes the three-class Otsu between-class variance to determine the optimal dual thresholds for oil film delineation. Experiments using X-band marine radar data collected during the Dalian 7·16 oil spill accident were conducted against seven representative methods. The proposed framework achieved a Precision of 97.20%, an F1-score of 86.60%, and an IoU of 76.40%, demonstrating superior oil film segmentation accuracy and effective false-alarm suppression under complex sea-clutter conditions. It also maintained a favorable balance between segmentation accuracy and computational efficiency, demonstrating its potential for marine oil spill monitoring under the investigated radar observation conditions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Sparse-view UAV imagery provides uneven multi-view supervision for 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS), allowing densification to increase model size without comparable reconstruction gains. We propose Structured Gaussian Dropout and Frozen Growth (SGDF) to control active and total primitive counts. Fixed Cardinality (FC) converts the dropout rate into an integer deactivation budget. Frozen Growth (FG) stops later additions and removals while continuous parameters continue to optimize. RISK-A ranks candidate primitives using training-state statistics after protection filtering. Across six scenes with 16, 32, and 48 training views, SGDF achieved mean PSNR, SSIM, and LPIPS values of 25.932 dB, 0.8061, and 0.1804. Relative to 3DGS, PSNR increased by 0.655 dB, while LPIPS decreased by 10.0%. On AeroGrid100-32, final primitive count and full training time decreased by 96.1% and 71.4%, respectively. In the LiDAR evaluation, Full SGDF reduced weighted camera-z RMSE by 15.6% relative to FG. SGDF improves average reconstruction quality across the evaluated UAV scenes while reducing model size and training time on AeroGrid100-32.

Abstract (英文): The expertly rectified extraction of cropland parcel boundaries is a critical basis for precision agriculture deployment and land resource management. However, the scarcity of large-scale, high-quality, cross-regional reference datasets limits the generalizability of deep learning models. To address this data bottleneck, we created a standardized, multi-source cropland parcel boundary benchmark dataset. The collection, which covers Europe (Denmark, the Netherlands, Spain) and Asia (Central China), encompasses over 180,000 expert-rectified parcels, with average parcel sizes ranging from 0.05 to 10 hectares and spatial resolutions spanning 0.2 to 10 m. Based on this dataset, we evaluated nine representative deep learning models belonging to three architectural paradigms (edge detection, multi-task semantic segmentation, and instance segmentation) and analyzed the effects of spatial resolution, parcel scale, and topographic relief on extraction accuracy. The experimental results indicate the following: (1) Spatial resolution exerts a non-linear influence on boundary extraction fidelity, where some medium-resolution data conditions (such as 4.8 m imagery in large-scale fields) retained competitive boundary extraction performance for certain models. (2) Even small but regular parcels of merely 0.22 hectares can be extracted with high accuracy, achieving a maximum F1 score of 86.58%. (3) Topographic variations lead to contrasting model performance. In plain and hilly terrain, multi-task semantic segmentation models exhibit obvious advantages. BsiNet yields the highest F1 score of 86.58%, but this value drops to 36.56% in complex mountainous regions. By comparison, edge detection models perform better in mountainous areas, where RCF attains the maximum F1 of 70.85%. In summary, this study establishes a standardized, expertly refined reference parcel dataset and provides an empirical evaluation of how different model architectures perform across varied observational settings, offering an empirical baseline for future model development and evaluation in cropland boundary extraction.

</details>

Abstract (英文): In general object detection tasks, accurate responses to the boundaries and classifications of potential objects are required of detectors. Given the diverse shapes of objects, these bounding boxes are typically represented as horizontal rectangles. However, in aerial imagery scenarios, horizontal bounding boxes can easily miss valid detections when objects are densely distributed. In recent years, oriented object detection algorithms have achieved considerable success. However, they focus on the boundaries of the object without taking into account its head–tail attributes and true orientation. Getting the true orientation of objects serves as the long-term aim of remote sensing object detection task. To resolve these issues, we decompose the single Gaussian distribution probability loss into a sequential dual-probability distribution, thereby establishing a one-to-one collaborative loss mechanism. This enables the model to learn the real orientation relationship from the dual distribution loss, achieving the acquisition of objects’ true orientation information. To more accurately reflect the preference of detectors, we design a new evaluation metric based on the cosine function and average precision. We conduct a series of experiments on the DOTA dataset, HRSC dataset, and FAIR1M dataset, achieving satisfactory results that validate the effectiveness of the proposed method.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Satellite-AOD-based visibility estimation has largely focused on daily-scale estimates or limited overpass times, while its diurnal variations are less understood and discussed. This study therefore investigates surface visibility estimations over China during daytime at 00, 03, 06, and 09 UTC (i.e., 08:00, 11:00, 14:00, and 17:00 China Standard Time, UTC+8), using aerosol optical depth (AOD) observations from the geostationary FY-4B satellite and ERA5 meteorological data. A physically informed framework combines a first-order physical visibility estimate based on AOD, an empirically calibrated relative humidity (RH) adjustment, and XGBoost residual learning. The complete framework, evaluated against ground-based NCEI visibility observations, achieved R = 0.84, RMSE = 2.80 km, and MAE = 2.28 km under sample-based five-fold cross-validation. Independent validation from July 2024 to June 2025 yielded R = 0.79, RMSE = 3.10 km, and MAE = 2.56 km. Visibility-estimation accuracy varied across daytime observation times, with relatively lower errors at 00 and 03 UTC and the largest errors at 06 UTC in both the first-order physical estimate and the final model. Although the complete framework yielded RMSE values of 2.63–3.68 km across the four time slots, these diurnal differences remained evident. The physical estimate also exhibited RH-dependent bias, overestimating visibility at lower RH and underestimating it at higher RH, with a transition near 50%. An empirical RH-conditioned adjustment further reduced estimation errors. These findings highlight the importance of accounting for diurnal and RH-dependent errors in simplified AOD-to-visibility conversion and demonstrate the potential of repeated geostationary observations for daytime surface visibility estimation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): In-season crop identification requires accurate, timely, and reliable predictions from asynchronous SAR and optical observations that accumulate progressively during early and middle calendar checkpoints. This study proposes a progressive SAR–optical fusion framework with selective risk control for parcel-level crop monitoring. A Gated Cross-Attention Temporal Network (GCAT) integrates complementary Sentinel-1 and Sentinel-2 time-series information, while random-prefix training and real day-of-year encoding accommodate asynchronous and unequal-length observations. Probability calibration and a sequential ACCEPT/DEFER strategy further allow uncertain predictions to be deferred until a predefined acceptance criterion is satisfied. Experiments on PASTIS-R show that GCAT achieves a full-season Macro-F1 of 80.57 ± 0.83% across five official folds, outperforming unimodal and multimodal fusion baselines. In the primary Fold-2 evaluation, GCAT provides larger gains than the Sentinel-2-only model at early- and mid-season operational checkpoints, reaches acceptance 7.55 days earlier on average among jointly accepted parcels, and increases accepted coverage. Cross-year evaluation on Brandenburg shows that the classification and earliness advantages of multimodal fusion are partially retained, whereas the confidence–risk relationship degrades markedly. These results highlight the value of SAR–optical fusion for earlier in-season decisions while emphasizing the need for renewed calibration and reliability assessment across years.

Abstract (英文): Assimilating satellite-retrieved profiles is an important approach to improving a numerical model’s initial conditions and typhoon forecasting skill. This study uses temperature and humidity retrieved profile products from the Geostationary Interferometric Infrared Sounder (GIIRS) onboard Fengyun-4B for three typhoons that affected Zhejiang Province in July 2025 with different track types. Sensitivity experiments are conducted under clear-sky, cloudy-sky, and all-sky conditions. Vortex relocation experiments are designed to separate the contributions of typhoon vortex structure and environmental field to track forecasts. Results show that assimilation systematically adjusts temperature and humidity in the typhoon inner core, rainband, and outer environment. All schemes produce lower–middle tropospheric warming and upper tropospheric cooling. Humidity decreases in the low-level outer environment but increases around the typhoon vortex and along the Western Pacific Subtropical High edge. For the three typhoon cases examined in this study, all-sky assimilation is optimal for track forecasts, whereas clear-sky or cloudy-sky assimilation alone can be adverse. For the 24 h accumulated precipitation during the period of severe typhoon impact on Zhejiang, all-sky assimilation increases the threat scores for heavy rain and torrential rain by 0.04 and 0.09, respectively, relative to the control experiment, and increases the extreme torrential rain threat score by 0.09 relative to cloudy-sky assimilation. For the three typhoon cases examined in this study, all-sky GIIRS profile assimilation shows clear advantages for typhoon forecasting and highlights the potential for the operational application of GIIRS-retrieved temperature and humidity profiles.

</details>

Abstract (英文): High-altitude lakes on the Tibetan Plateau (TP) are important components of regional water-cycle regulation and climate responses, yet their long-term monitoring remains challenging due to limited in situ observations and sparse spatial sampling of conventional satellite altimetry. The Surface Water and Ocean Topography (SWOT) mission provides two-dimensional observations of inland water-surface elevation (WSE), offering new opportunities for lake monitoring in remote regions. This study evaluates the applicability of SWOT L2 Pixel Cloud (PIXC) observations for TP lake monitoring through cross-sensor comparison with ICESat-2 ATL13 observations. A total of 50 representative TP lakes were analyzed using quality-controlled SWOT–ICESat-2 matched observations. The results demonstrate that SWOT PIXC and ICESat-2 ATL13 generally capture comparable lake-scale WSE variations across the TP. The mean MAE and RMSE were 0.164 m and 0.231 m, respectively, indicating decimeter-scale cross-sensor consistency for most investigated lakes. Lake area showed no significant association with the RMSE, suggesting that lake size alone does not explain the variability of cross-sensor residuals. Further analysis reveals that residual differences are strongly scene-dependent and reflect combined influences from satellite retrieval characteristics, spatial sampling differences, environmental conditions, and temporal offsets rather than SWOT-only uncertainty. These findings provide an independent assessment of SWOT observations over high-altitude lakes and demonstrate their potential for spatially continuous monitoring of TP lake dynamics. The results also establish a foundation for integrating SWOT with multi-source satellite observations to reconstruct long-term lake water-level and storage variations.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Fine-crack segmentation in high-resolution UAV images of bridges is hindered by weak crack signals, complex backgrounds, and substantial computational demands. This study proposes a dual-stage framework combining lightweight grid-wise screening with graph-enhanced segmentation. CrackClsNet identifies candidate regions, and CrackSegNet processes contextual patches comprising each candidate and its eight neighbors. Multiscale and deformable convolutions, attention, and cross-patch graph reasoning support feature extraction, while overlapping predictions are fused during reconstruction. In experiments using 1000 pixel-annotated crack-containing images collected from 13 highway bridges and 500 crack-free images, neighborhood retention increased candidate region crack-pixel coverage from 91.97% to 95.65% on the classification test set. The end-to-end framework achieved an F1-score of 0.5826, Crack IoU of 0.4110, and mIoU of 0.7052, exceeding six baseline methods in these metrics. The results of ablation experiments further support the benefits of neighboring context and cross-patch feature exchange, with 3 × 3 neighborhoods balancing segmentation quality and computational cost. With approximately 4.41 million parameters, the framework achieved 4.10 FPS with a reported memory consumption of 6.94 GB at 4096 × 5632 pixels, providing the highest throughput and lowest reported memory consumption among the evaluated methods at this resolution. These results indicate a promising balance between segmentation performance and computational cost on the evaluated bridge image dataset.

Abstract (英文): LiDAR–camera fusion for 3D object detection combines the metric geometry of LiDAR measurements with the semantic information in images. However, projected LiDAR depth is sparse and provides incomplete geometric guidance to the image branch, while the modality-specific branches follow different learning dynamics during multimodal training. We present Co-Fusion, a LiDAR-dominant collaborative fusion framework that extends the previously published Co-Fix3D detector. Co-Fusion introduces a LiDAR-depth guidance pipeline that standardizes projected LiDAR depth using dataset-level statistics and encodes the sparse measurements into features aligned with the image branch. A gated fusion network applies multiplicative modulation to the joint image–depth representation to produce depth-aware camera features. A three-stage parameter-transfer training schedule selectively transfers parameters from the LiDAR branch to initialize the camera and fusion branches. Experiments on the nuScenes 3D detection benchmark show that Co-Fusion reaches 74.1% mAP and 75.3% NDS in the single-frame multimodal setting without test-time augmentation.

Abstract (英文): UAV patrol plays a crucial role in combating illegal poppy cultivation. However, the complex and diverse environments in which poppies are cultivated demand effective local and global feature modeling to improve detection performance while keeping computational costs low. Existing CNN–Transformer methods often rely on complex feature interaction to exploit their complementary strengths, and the scarcity of publicly available poppy datasets further restricts the development of poppy detection methods. To address the above issues, we construct an Independent CNN–Transformer Dual-Branch network (ICTNet), which separately models the CNN branch and the Transformer branch, thereby avoiding complex feature interaction. For the Transformer branch, we design a Gated Transformer Block (GTB). GTB employs gated linear attention to model complex spatial information with linear computational complexity, achieving a favorable balance between detection performance and computational efficiency. For the CNN branch, we design a Progressive Convolution Block (PCB) with an expanding receptive field, which effectively models local features while capturing rich contextual information. Moreover, we create a multi-scene poppy detection dataset named MS-Poppy, which contains 2443 images covering six UAV-view scenes. Extensive experiments are conducted on the MS-Poppy and the public GWHD 2021 dataset. Experimental results indicate that, compared with the advanced YOLO12, ICTNet reduces parameters by 11% while improving mAP@0.5 by 5.4% on the MS-Poppy dataset and by 1.0% on the GWHD 2021 dataset, respectively.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Urban agglomerations exhibit pronounced ecological heterogeneity due to complex interactions between anthropogenic and natural factors. However, most existing studies assess ecological conditions from an aggregated regional perspective, potentially obscuring localized ecological mechanisms and heterogeneous ecological responses among different functional areas. To address this issue, an integrated ecological assessment framework incorporating anthropogenic indicators was developed to investigate the spatiotemporal heterogeneity and driving mechanisms of ecological conditions across major function-oriented zones in the Beijing–Tianjin–Hebei region from 2001 to 2020. The results revealed significant functional-zone-dependent heterogeneity in ecological responses. The ecological conservation area maintained the highest ecological conditions with continuous improvement, largely attributed to extensive vegetation coverage and ecological restoration efforts. In contrast, the core function area showed relatively low ecological conditions, primarily constrained by urban thermal stress, atmospheric pollution, and intensive human activities. The coastal development area displayed fragmented ecological patterns, with ecological responses influenced jointly by climatic conditions and socioeconomic activities. The secondary function area exhibited more complex ecological dynamics, where ecological conditions were shaped by the interaction of land cover, GDP, and natural factors. These findings demonstrate that ecological responses within urban agglomerations are fundamentally shaped by functional-zone-dependent anthropogenic–natural coupling processes. By integrating anthropogenic indicators into ecological assessment and examining ecological dynamics from a functional-zone perspective, the proposed framework enables the identification of localized ecological mechanisms and heterogeneous driver–response relationships that may be obscured by aggregated regional analyses. Furthermore, the proposed framework provides scientific support for functional-zone-oriented ecological governance, differentiated ecological restoration, and sustainable regional development.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Infrared imagery supports low-light target perception, but acquiring, registering, and annotating paired visible–infrared imagery is costly. Visible-to-infrared image translation can alleviate this demand, yet cross-modal differences often degrade object boundaries and spatial layouts, especially for small targets. We propose DFSMamba to preserve geometric structure throughout visible-to-infrared translation by connecting frequency-guided feature formation, input-dependent directional aggregation, and infrared-domain reconstruction supervision. A discrete wavelet transform (DWT)-based detail-guided module supplements bottleneck features with learned structural cues, direction-adaptive 2D selective scan adjusts the fusion of aligned directional responses, and state-space similarity matching constrains the output in a fixed multiscale feature space learned through real-infrared reconstruction. Experiments on FMB, VEDAI, and AVIID-1 include image-quality comparisons, target-level evaluation, ablation studies, efficiency analysis, and downstream vehicle detection. Among evaluated methods, DFSMamba achieves the best SSIM, MS-SSIM, PSNR, and L1 values on all three datasets. On AVIID-1, it obtains an SSIM of 0.985 and a PSNR of 41.799 dB. A YOLOv9-C detector trained on generated images reaches an AP50:95 of 0.636 on real infrared test images, compared with 0.640 when trained on real infrared images. These results show that DFSMamba preserves detection-relevant structure and supports the use of generated images as synthetic training data in the evaluated aerial vehicle-detection setting.

Abstract (英文): The inherent class imbalance and scarcity of labeled samples remain major challenges in hyperspectral image (HSI) classification. SMOTE-based methods alleviate class imbalance by generating synthetic minority-class samples. However, their interpolation mechanisms often fail to capture spatial–spectral features adequately, resulting in noisy or boundary-ambiguous samples. To address this issue, this paper proposes Spatial–Spectral Prototype-guided Deep SMOTE (SSPD) for imbalanced HSI classification. Firstly, SSPD constructs a spatial–spectral representation guided by feature importance to suppress redundant spectral bands and isolated spatial noise. Secondly, SSPD employs a prototype-guided oversampling strategy to generate synthetic minority-class samples. Class prototypes determine the generation direction, while feature-importance weights constrain the interpolation process to preserve discriminative spectral responses. Finally, a stepwise ensemble with soft voting is employed to integrate classifiers trained under different imbalance ratios. Extensive experiments were conducted on Indian Pines, Pavia University, Salinas, and WHU-Hi benchmark datasets. Compared with the individual machine-learning classifiers, SSPD achieves more stable and competitive performance in terms of Overall Accuracy (OA), Average Accuracy (AA), and Kappa Coefficient (KC).

</details>

Abstract (英文): Long-term satellite observations of cloud amount provide critical constraints on climate sensitivity and are essential for reducing uncertainties in climate projections. Different cloud types exert distinct radiative effects and exhibit divergent responses to warming. Yet, their relative contributions to trends in total cloud amount remain inadequately quantified. This study analyzes 17 years (2003–2019) of MODIS observations and reveals that, although global and zonal (60°S–60°N) mean trends in total cloud amount are weak, significant local signals persist. Trend decomposition demonstrates that free-tropospheric cloud occurrence dominates the magnitude of total cloud amount trends, whereas low-level cloud (LowC) size primarily dictates their spatial distribution. Nevertheless, this LowC size signal is substantially suppressed by an opposing LowC occurrence signal, resulting in a significant decoupling between LowC and total cloud amounts. To address the inherent limitations of passive satellite retrievals, we further utilize ERA5 reanalysis data. This data further indicates that approximately half of the trend in LowC occurrence arises from cloud overlap biases unresolved by passive satellite sensors. Correcting for this bias should yield a coherent shift in LowC amount trends over most oceans, suggesting that passive satellite retrievals may have systematically obscured the response of low-level clouds to recent climate change.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Soil bidirectional reflectance distribution function (BRDF) is strongly influenced by soil physicochemical properties, yet most hyperspectral retrieval studies rely on single-view reflectance, leaving the value of multi-angle information insufficiently explored. In this study, bidirectional reflectance factors (BRFs) were measured at 48 viewing angles for 156 soil samples collected from a region dominated by Ali-Perudic Argosols. An optimal multi-angle retrieval framework was developed for different soil properties by systematically comparing spectral preprocessing methods, feature extraction, and modeling algorithms. The effects of viewing angle on retrieval accuracy and feature wavelength distribution were further analyzed, and the applicability of simulated multi-angle BRFs generated using SOILSPECT model was further assessed. Results show that the optimal framework for single-view reflectance combines Savitzky–Golay (SG) smoothing, a hybrid random frog–competitive adaptive reweighted sampling (RFROG–CARS) feature selection strategy, and nonlinear support vector machine (SVM) regression. Retrieval accuracy varied substantially with viewing geometry, and no universal optimal viewing angle was identified across soil properties. For multi-angle BRF data, a two-dimensional convolutional neural network (2D-CNN) feature extraction approach outperformed the RFROG–CARS wavelength selection method. Specifically, R 2 (RMSE) improved from 0.64 (8.8 g/kg) to 0.73 (7.7 g/kg) for SOM content, from 0.53 (6.9 %) to 0.61 (6.3 %) for sand content, and from 0.64 (4.7 %) to 0.69 (4.4 %) for silt content, while remaining comparable for cation exchange capacity (0.77/2.4 cmol/kg to 0.76/2.4 cmol/kg) and clay content (0.80/4.3 % to 0.79/4.3 %). Although the retrieval accuracy based on multi-angle BRFs remained slightly lower than that obtained from the optimal viewing geometry, it consistently outperformed most individual viewing angles. The selected multi-angle hyperspectral features were distributed across nearly all viewing angles, indicating complementary spectral–geometric information. Although retrieval accuracy based on simulated BRFs was lower than that based on measured BRFs, the results were broadly comparable. These findings support the use of multi-angle information in soil spectroscopy and remote sensing applications.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate estimation of leaf nitrogen content (LNC) and grain protein content (GPC) in winter wheat is crucial for making fertilization recommendations and assessing grain quality. Previous methods primarily relied on chlorophyll (Chl)-sensitive vegetation indices from the visible and near-infrared domain, while shortwave-infrared spectral features linked to dry matter and nitrogen remain underutilized due to strong leaf water masking. Although existing water removal methods can mitigate the absorption effects of leaf water, the reliability of the resulting simulated dry-canopy reflectance spectra (SDR) is limited by inaccurate leaf mass per area (LMA) retrieval. This study aimed to develop a semi-empirical water removal approach by integrating PRior knowledge and INitialization of leaf water conTent and LMA (PRINT) to simulate SDR, with the objective of improving the estimation performance of LNC and GPC. To achieve this goal, a Random Forest-based hybrid retrieval model was developed to increase LMA retrieval accuracy. Meanwhile, a visible and shortwave-based nitrogen index (VSNI) by combining the visible (proxy of Chl) and shortwave-infrared features was proposed for improving estimation performance of LNC and GPC. Moreover, experimental datasets covering multiple growth stages and geographic sites were collected to improve sample representativeness. These datasets were utilized to improve the retrieval of LMA and evaluate the performance of LNC and GPC estimations. The results demonstrated that the PRINT significantly improved the estimation accuracy of LMA over two standard physically-based inversion models (RMSE: 6.23 g/m 2 vs 8.25 g/m 2 and 45.73 g/m 2 ). Compared with existing VI-based models, the VSNI-based models yielded substantial improvements in LNC (R 2 : 0.62 ∼ 0.65, RMSE = 0.42 ∼ 0.54%) across sites, and enhanced GPC estimation accuracy up to R 2 = 0.66 (RMSE = 0.70%) at the peak filling stage across two years at one site. The proposed methodology advances the spectroscopic understanding of shortwave-infrared responses to nitrogen-related agronomic biochemicals, and holds significant potential for future applications in large-scale growth monitoring of wheat systems using satellite imagery.

</details>

This data gap constrains investigation of intra-urban heat variability and the timing of peak heat stress. Here, based on the Rankine-Kirchhoff approximations of Romps, this study innovatively generates hourly 1-km T w by integrating crowdsourced station observations with remote sensing and reanalysis data in machine learning based downscaling frameworks. Taking London during the summers of 2019–2022 as a case study, the tuned RF-based downscaling approach achieved the best performance ( MAE = 0.65 °C, RMSE = 0.93 °C, R 2 = 0.91) among all models. The resulting T w patterns diverge from those commonly reported for T a : blue infrastructure shows no consistent cooling effect, whereas vegetated areas cool T w increasingly as tree cover rises, pointing to tree-dominated green infrastructure as a promising humid-heat countermeasure. Results further show that daily maximum T w occurs 0.7 h earlier than daily maximum T a in London, opposite to findings elsewhere, implying that climate type and coastal influence modulate the timing of peak T w , which may affect the attribution of excess nighttime mortality. Besides, a systematic global assessment of the mechanisms controlling T w extremes is also required in the future.

Abstract (英文): Lake ice phenology is a sensitive indicator of climate change, yet its regional hydroclimatic associations remain poorly understood. Here, we reconstructed daily lake surface water temperature (LSWT) from MODIS for 583 lakes across China and derived freeze-up date (FUD), break-up date (BUD), and ice cover duration (ICD) for 376 seasonally ice-covered lakes. Robustness was evaluated with sensitivity tests, meteorological observations, transition-period comparisons, and passive-microwave ice records; Mann-Kendall tests, Theil-Sen slopes, VIF-constrained XGBoost-SHAP, and structural equation modeling assessed trends and regional associations. FUD was delayed in all three ice-covered regions, whereas BUD was delayed in the Tibetan Plateau Lake Region (TPR) but advanced in the Mongolian-Xinjiang Lake Region (MXR) and Northeast Lake Region (NLR), resulting in shorter ICD in all regions. Regional mean ICD declined by 0.03, 0.35, and 0.47 d yr -1 in the TPR, MXR, and NLR, respectively; significant lake-level changes were concentrated in the TPR, with 72 and 33 lakes showing significant FUD and BUD trends. All nine models retained positive hold-out R 2 (0.535–0.960). SHAP attributions differed by region: radiative variables dominated in the TPR, the MXR showed metric-specific associations with wind, snowfall, geographic, and thermal , and geographic, radiative, and morphometric variables dominated in the NLR. Structural equation models indicated stronger precipitation-related associations in the NLR and snowfall-related associations in the MXR and TPR. These results reveal pronounced regional heterogeneity in lake ice responses to warming and the value of integrating remote sensing, interpretable machine learning, and pathway modeling to resolve these contrasts.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Spaceborne Global Navigation Satellite System Reflectometry (GNSS-R) has shown great potential for terrestrial monitoring, but the compensation of small-scale surface roughness remains an important challenge. In particular, the root-mean-square surface height σ h , its incidence angle dependence represented by n in c o s n θ , and their relationships with soil moisture (SM) are still insufficiently characterized for spaceborne GNSS-R. In this study, CYGNSS surface reflectivity (SR) observations are combined with model-simulated smooth bare-soil reflectivity derived from SMOS SM and vegetation attenuation represented by SMOS-IC L-band Vegetation Optical Depth (L-VOD). Global σ h and n are estimated on a 25 km grid by fitting the model predictions to CYGNSS observations. The retrieved σ h is compared to multiple topographic variables, SMAP and SMOS-IC roughness parameters, and Saharan aerodynamic roughness measurements, while the dependence of n on vegetation and land cover is also investigated. Results show that the retrieved σ h is more strongly associated with terrain descriptors such as uniformity, Shannon index, entropy, Northness, and Eastness than with conventional elevation- and slope-based variables. Both σ h and n also exhibit relationships with SM and vegetation, indicating potential coupling between effective roughness characterization and vegetation-related uncertainties. After compensation for vegetation and roughness effects, CYGNSS SR shows good consistency with simulated bare soil SR. Additional evaluation using in situ SM observations from the International Soil Moisture Network further supports the effectiveness of the retrieved parameters. Overall, these findings offer valuable insights into the use of physics-based empirical or semi-empirical models in spaceborne GNSS-R for soil moisture retrievals and other terrestrial applications.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Despite significant progress in point cloud–based 3D building reconstruction, the effectiveness of existing methods remains constrained by four major bottlenecks. These include the scarcity of high-quality annotated datasets, the pervasive incompleteness and noise in real-world point clouds, the substantial memory consumption incurred by the edge search space, and the lack of adequate geometric constraints in network designs, which often causes loss functions to fail in capturing the underlying 3D structural regularities. To address these challenges, we pre-train a self-supervised learning (SSL) framework on large-scale unlabeled point clouds to learn generic geometric representations, and subsequently fine-tune it using only a limited set of annotated roof samples for 3D wireframe reconstruction. The key contribution of this work is the proposal of a new SSL-based framework that utilizes geometry aware edge point identification to facilitate wireframe reconstruction and avoid missing corners, thereby mitigating issues related to dataset quality and computational complexity. Specifically, the framework first leverages a pretrained SSL-based feature extractor to generate robust point-wise representations under sparse and imperfect inputs. Building on these features, an edge point–guided regression module predicts the parametric attributes of roof wireframes. To effectively supervise the training of this regression module, we formulate a joint loss function that optimizes two complementary objectives: (1) minimizing the Euclidean distance between input points and their nearest line segments, and (2) reducing the distance between candidate edge points and their projections on the reconstructed model. Coupled with bipartite edge matching and an edge-aware non-maximum suppression strategy, the proposed method ensures geometric validity while reducing computational redundancy. Extensive experiments across three datasets of varying geometric complexity and scale (Dense, Building3D, and the large-scale Tallinn City dataset) demonstrate the framework’s effectiveness. The SSL-based pretrained model attains 97% corner precision and 91% edge precision for wireframe reconstruction on the Building3D dataset, surpassing conventional baselines and approaching the performance of fully supervised methods. Remarkably, even under limited supervision, specifically utilizing only 50% partially labeled data during fine-tuning, the model maintains strong accuracy with 86% corner precision and 67% edge precision, underscoring its advantages over current SSL-based approaches.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent advances in remote sensing (RS) have improved panchromatic (PAN) and multispectral (MS) data acquisition, but challenges remain in multi-source fusion classification due to limitations in non-redundant feature extraction (i.e., effectively preserving complementary information while suppressing source-specific redundancies) and cross-source alignment (i.e., accurately matching spatial–spectral correspondences between PAN and MS data). The emerging state space model (SSM)-based Mamba model, with its global modeling and linear complexity, shows potential for complex land cover classification tasks. To this end, we propose MMC-Net, a novel Mamba-based multi-level class-aware network for PAN and MS fusion classification, which integrates a progressive fusion feature learning (PFFL) module and a multi-branch class-aware classification (MBCAC) module. The PFFL module employs dual-path spatial–spectral Mamba (S 2 Mamba) to extract discriminative features while suppressing redundancy through the feature reconstruction module (FRM), along with fusion Mamba (FMamba) to enable cross-source interaction via channel exchange and Fusion SSM. The MBCAC module enhances classification diversity through spatial-wise softmax and multi-branch learning, while its parameter-sharing mechanism across levels captures hierarchical discriminative patterns. Validated on three PAN-MS and one HSI-LiDAR datasets, MMC-Net achieves state-of-the-art performance and demonstrates robust, source-agnostic generalization across heterogeneous modalities. Abstract (英文): Mountain vegetation dynamics serve as sensitive indicators of environmental change, making accurate fractional cover mapping of primary components crucial for understanding ecosystem responses. This task has been facilitated by multispectral satellite imagery, particularly Sentinel-2, which provides continuous observations. To translate these observations into fractional cover maps, existing studies typically apply fraction estimation models to synthetic spectra derived from field-measured signatures. However, limited field surveys in mountain terrain present a data bottleneck for synthetic spectra construction, while complex vegetation composition introduces spectral ambiguities that existing methods struggle to represent. Therefore, this study develops a knowledge-guided unmixing framework for annual mapping of the fractional cover of herbaceous vegetation, woody vegetation, and bare land in mountain environments using Sentinel-2 time series. Specifically, to overcome data bottleneck, we develop a knowledge-guided strategy that automatically builds adaptive physio-phenological feature spaces to extract representative signatures. To resolve spectral ambiguity, we incorporate nonlinear mixing mechanisms to synthesize spectra that better represent complex mixing relationships. The synthetic library was then used to train Unmix-Net, a spectral-temporal regression model for fraction estimation. The framework was applied to the central Qilian Mountains and southern Hengduan Mountains spanning broad climatic gradients, validated with high-resolution UAV data. It achieved MAEs of 12.38 %, 8.58 %, and 9.82 % for herbaceous, woody, and bare land, outperforming existing models by up to 7.80 %. When implemented across landscapes ranging from arid steppes and alpine meadows to tropical rainforests, the framework maintained comparable performance. The comparative experiments further confirmed that the proposed extraction strategy improved the balance of component estimates, while combining complementary mixing mechanisms reduced estimation errors. By integrating domain knowledge into data-driven learning, this framework supports spatially continuous fractional vegetation mapping in mountain environments.

</details>

### [465] [A physics-constrained superpixel-based multi-source remote sensing spatiotemporal fusion network](https://doi.org/10.1080/15481603.2026.2741478)
*Qinyu Zhao,Luyan Ji,Qingxue Wang,Yaoxin Zhang,Yonggang Ma,Liqiang Zhang*

Abstract (英文): Remote sensing spatiotemporal fusion aims to generate image sequences with both high spatial and temporal resolution. However, many deep learning approaches treat fusion as a black-box regression problem, overlooking differences in sensor imaging physics—such as the point spread function (PSF) and pixel-area integration—which often leads to boundary blurring, spectral mixing, or even physically implausible high-frequency details over heterogeneous landscapes. In addition, these methods commonly rely on paired coarse-/fine-resolution reference images at the same time, which limits data availability. To address these issues, we propose SPP-STF, a superpixel-based fusion network trained with a physics-guided degradation-consistency regularizer that requires only a reference fine-resolution image and a target coarse-resolution observation, while deriving an edge intensity map as a structural prior. The model adopts a U-Net-style, three-branch encoder–decoder to fuse texture, temporal variation, and edge cues at multiple scales. An EdgeResBlock uses high-frequency gating to sharpen boundaries and reduce cross-class leakage, and a superpixel cross-attention Transformer learns object-level correspondences to alleviate alignment errors caused by mixed pixels. During training, we introduce a learnable anisotropic Gaussian PSF-based degradation-consistency term: the predicted fine-resolution output is blurred and downsampled via area integration to match the observed coarse image, and this term is optimized jointly with the reconstruction loss. Quantitatively, SPP-STF achieves the best RMSE, SAM, PSNR, and UIQI on CIA (0.0254, 0.1522, 31.9330 dB, and 0.8451), the best results across all six metrics on LGC (including RMSE 0.0188 and PSNR 34.6307 dB), and the best RMSE, SSIM, SAM, PSNR, and ERGAS on Tianjin (0.0410, 0.7640, 0.2459, 27.8666 dB, and 30.4838). Ablation studies confirm the complementary benefits of the superpixel and edge modules. The PSF term is interpreted here as a training-time regularizer that provides consistent and physically more reasonable improvements across the three datasets. Structured pruning further indicates that the model can retain spatial structure under substantial compression, while spectral fidelity becomes notably more sensitive beyond roughly 70% pruning.

</details>
